F^{\prime}:={\rm pr}_{1}\circ\rho\colon Y\to X^{\prime},\qquad F:=\beta\circ F^{\prime}=\beta\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\circ\rho\colon Y\to X.

Since
(F^{*}\xi,h_{F^{*}\xi}:=F^{*}h_{\xi})
is Nakano semi-positive,
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)
is a locally free
{\mathcal{O}}_{T}
-module
by Lemma
2.2
. Since
f|_{Y\setminus Y_{0}}\colon Y\setminus Y_{0}\to T^{o}
is
G
-equivariant,

R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)|_{T^{o}}
is viewed as a
G
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundle on
T^{o}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

The
G
-action on
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)|_{T^{o}}
extends to a holomorphic
G
-action on
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)
.

Proof.

Let
\rho^{\prime\prime}\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X\times_{S}T
be a
G
-equivariant resolution and set
\varpi^{\prime\prime}:={\rm pr}_{2}\circ\rho^{\prime\prime}
and

\varPi^{\prime\prime}:={\rm pr}_{1}\circ\rho^{\prime\prime}
.
By the
G
-equivariance of
\varpi^{\prime\prime}\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to T
,
R^{q}\varpi^{\prime\prime}_{*}K_{X^{\prime\prime}}(\varPi^{{}^{\prime\prime}*}\xi)
is a
G
-equivariant holomorphic
vector bundle on
T
. Since
Y
is birational to
X^{\prime\prime}
, considering a resolution of
X\times_{S}T
dominating both of
X^{\prime\prime}
and
Y
,
we get an isomorphism
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)\cong R^{q}\varpi^{\prime\prime}_{*}K_{X^{\prime\prime}}(\varPi^{{}^{\prime\prime}*}\xi)
of holomorphic vector bundles
on
T
by
[
46
, Th. 6.9 (i)]
, which induces the desired
G
-action on
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)
.
∎

4.2.
Set up

Let
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}
be the Kähler form of
h_{\mathcal{X}}
and set
\kappa_{X}:=\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}|_{X}
.
We write
t
for the coordinate of
T\cong\varDelta
centered at
0
and we set
\kappa_{T}:=i\,dt\wedge d\bar{t}
.
Then
\kappa_{T}
is a Kähler form on
T
. Define

\kappa_{Y}:=F^{*}\kappa_{X}+f^{*}\kappa_{T}.

Then
\kappa_{Y}
is a smooth
(1,1)
-form on
Y
, which is a Kähler form only on
Y\setminus Y_{0}
.
To understand the asymptotic behavior of the
L^{2}
-metric on
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)
near
t=0
with respect to
the
degenerate
Kähler form
\kappa_{Y}
, we need an analogue of Theorem
2.1
for
the Nakano semi-positive vector bundle
(F^{*}\xi,F^{*}h_{\xi})
on
(Y,\kappa_{Y})
, as well as a bound from below of the
L^{2}
-metric.
Such an extension of Theorem
2.1
and an estimate for the
L^{2}
-metric were obtained by
Mourougane-Takayama
[
36
, Prop. 4.4]
,
[
36
, Lemmas 4.7 and 4.8]
.
However, their results are not directly applicable to our setting for the following reasons. Set

\varSigma^{\prime}:={\rm Sing}(X^{\prime}\times_{S}T),\qquad U^{\prime}:=(X^{\prime}\times_{S}T)\setminus\varSigma^{\prime}.

We may assume by
[
28
, Chap. II]
that
\rho\colon Y\setminus\rho^{-1}(\varSigma)\to U^{\prime}
is an isomorphism and that

\rho^{-1}(\varSigma)\subset Y_{0}
is a normal crossing divisor. However, it is not clear from the construction in
[
28
, Chap. II]

if
\rho\colon Y\to X^{\prime}\times_{S}T
is a
composite of blowing-ups with non-singular centers
disjoint from
U^{\prime}
.
Since this condition is essential in the construction of a sequence of Kähler forms on
Y
approximating
\kappa_{Y}

(cf.
[
36
, Proof of Prop. 4.4 Step 1]
), we are not able to apply the arguments in
[
36
, Prop. 4.4]
to the bundle
(F^{*}\xi,h_{F^{*}\xi})
on
(Y,\kappa_{Y})
at once.
Another point is that the non-vanishing
[
36
, Lemmas 4.7 and 4.8]
was proved
under the assumption that the metric on
Y
is non-degenerate (when
\dim S=1
).
Since
\kappa_{Y}
is degenerate on some components of
Y_{0}
, this non-vanishing result is not applicable to our setting.
We proceed as follows to resolve these problems.

For the first point, in stead of applying the argument of Mourougane-Takayama to
(Y,\kappa_{Y})
, we apply it to a manifold
Z

dominating
Y
, whose construction is as follows. By
[
5
, Th. 13.2]
, there exists a resolution of singularity

r\colon W\to X^{\prime}\times_{S}T
, which is a composite of blowing-ups with non-singular centers disjoint from
U^{\prime}
.
We apply
[
1
, Lemma 1.3.1]
by setting
X_{1}=W
and
X_{2}=Y
. Consequently, there exist a resolution

\widetilde{\rho}\colon Z\to X^{\prime}\times_{S}T

and a birational morphism

\varphi\colon Z\to Y

such that
\widetilde{\rho}
is a composite of blowing-ups with non-singular centers disjoint from
U^{\prime}
and

\widetilde{\rho}=\rho\circ\varphi.

In particular,

Z\setminus\widetilde{\rho}^{-1}(\varSigma^{\prime})\cong Y\setminus\rho^{-1}(\varSigma^{\prime})\cong U^{\prime}
and
Z_{0}={\varphi}^{-1}(Y_{0})
is a normal crossing divisor.
We set

(4.2)

\varpi:={\rm pr}_{2}\circ\widetilde{\rho}\colon Z\to T,\qquad\widetilde{F}:=\beta\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\circ\widetilde{\rho}\colon Z\to X.

Then

\widetilde{F}=F\circ\varphi\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varpi=f\circ\varphi.

In Section
4.3
, following Mourougane-Takayama
[
36
, Prop. 4.4]
, we prove a version of
Theorem
2.1
for the vector bundle
K_{Z}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi)
on
Z
with respect to a degenerate metric,
which leads to a corresponding result for the vector bundle
K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)
on
Y
.
Once we obtain a Takegoshi type theorem for the cohomology group
H^{q}(Y,K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi))
, we prove that the Hermitian matrix of
the
L^{2}
-metric
(\cdot,\cdot)_{L^{2},Y_{t}}
with respect to a holomorphic frame of
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)
admits
an asymptotic expansion of Barlet-Takayama type (Section
4.4
).
To prove the non-degeneracy of the
L^{2}
-metric on
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)
, which is exactly the second point mentioned above,
we fix a Kähler form
\kappa^{\prime}_{Y}
on
Y^{\prime}
with
\kappa_{Y}\leq\kappa^{\prime}_{Y}
, which yields
the corresponding
L^{2}
-metric
(\cdot,\cdot)^{\prime}_{L^{2},Y_{t}}
on
H^{q}(Y_{t},K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))
.
In Section
4.5
, we compare the metrics
(\cdot,\cdot)_{L^{2},Y_{t}}
and
(\cdot,\cdot)^{\prime}_{L^{2},Y_{t}}
to prove that the
non-degeneracy result
[
36
, Lemmas 4.7, 4.8]
for
(\cdot,\cdot)^{\prime}_{L^{2},Y_{t}}
implies a non-degeneracy result
for the metric
(\cdot,\cdot)_{L^{2},Y_{t}}
.

4.3.
Representation of cohomology classes by harmonic forms

Proposition 4.2
.

For every cohomology class
\Theta\in H^{q}(Y,K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi))
,
there exists a holomorphic differential form
\theta\in H^{0}(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{n+1-q}(F^{*}\xi))
such that

\Theta|_{Y\setminus Y_{0}}=[\theta\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}]|_{Y\setminus Y_{0}}

as elements of
H^{q}(Y\setminus Y_{0},K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi))
and

\theta\wedge f^{*}dt=0,\qquad D^{F^{*}\xi,F^{*}h_{\xi}}\theta=0.

Proof.

We follow
[
36
, Prop. 4.4]
.
Since the assertion is obvious for
q=0
, we suppose
q>0
. Then
\varphi^{*}\Theta\in H^{q}(Z,K_{Z}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))
.
Define

\kappa_{Z}:=\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\beta^{*}\kappa_{X}+({\rm pr}_{2})^{*}\kappa_{T}),

which is a degenerate Kähler form on
Z
with
\kappa_{Z}=\varphi^{*}\kappa_{Y}
.
Since
Z
is obtained from
X^{\prime}\times_{S}T
by a composite of blowing-ups with non-singular centers,
we deduce from e.g.
[
16
, Prop. 12.4]
,
[
36
, Proof of Prop. 4.4 Step 1]
that
there exists a sequence of Kähler forms
\{\kappa_{Z,k}\}_{k\geq 1}
on
Z
converging to
\kappa_{Z}
uniformly on
Z

such that
\kappa_{Z,k}=\kappa_{Z}
on
Z\setminus\varpi^{-1}(T(\frac{1}{k}))
.
By Theorem
2.1
applied to the Nakano semi-positive vector bundle
(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi,\widetilde{F}^{*}h_{\xi})

on the Kähler manifold
(Z,\kappa_{Z,k})
,
there is a holomorphic differential form
\theta_{k}\in\Gamma(Z,\Omega_{Z}^{n+1-q}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))
with

(4.3)

\varphi^{*}\Theta=[\theta_{k}\wedge\kappa_{Z,k}^{q}],\qquad(\varpi^{*}dt)\wedge\theta_{k}=0,\qquad D^{\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi,F^{*}h_{\xi}}\theta_{k}=0.

Here
D^{\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi,F^{*}h_{\xi}}
denotes the Chern connection of
(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi,\widetilde{F}^{*}h_{\xi})
.
Let
O\subset T
be an open subset such that
O\subset T\setminus T(\frac{1}{k})
for all
k>1
.
Since
\kappa_{Z,k}=\kappa_{Z}
on
Z\setminus\varpi^{-1}(T(\frac{1}{k}))
, we get
\kappa_{Z,k}=\kappa_{Z}
on
\varpi^{-1}(O)
.
By
[
46
, Th. 5.2 (iv)]
,
[
36
, Proof of Prop. 4.4 Step 3]
,
the equality
\kappa_{Z,k}|_{\varpi^{-1}(O)}=\kappa_{Z,l}|_{\varpi^{-1}(O)}
yields that
\theta_{k}|_{\varpi^{-1}(O)}=\theta_{l}|_{\varpi^{-1}(O)}
.
Hence
\theta_{k}=\theta_{l}
for all
k,l>1
. We set
\theta_{\infty}:=\theta_{k}
. By (
4.3
), for all
k>1
, we have

(4.4)

\varphi^{*}\Theta=[\theta_{\infty}\wedge\kappa_{Z,k}^{q}],\qquad(\varpi^{*}dt)\wedge\theta_{\infty}=0,\qquad D^{\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi,\widetilde{F}^{*}h_{\xi}}\theta_{\infty}=0.

Since
\kappa_{Z,k}=\kappa_{Z}
on
Z\setminus\varpi^{-1}(T(\frac{1}{k}))
, we get the following equality of cohomology classes:

(4.5)

\varphi^{*}\Theta|_{Z\setminus\varpi^{-1}(T(\frac{1}{k}))}=[\theta_{\infty}\wedge\kappa_{Z}^{q}]|_{Z\setminus\varpi^{-1}(T(\frac{1}{k}))}\qquad(k>1).

Set
Z_{0}:=\varpi^{-1}(0)
.
Let
\sigma_{\varphi^{*}\Theta},\sigma_{[\theta_{\infty}\wedge\kappa_{Z}^{q}]}\in\Gamma(T^{o},R^{q}\varpi_{*}K_{Z}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))

be the holomorphic sections corresponding to
\varphi^{*}\Theta|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}
and

[\theta_{\infty}\wedge\kappa_{Z}^{q}]|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}
, respectively.
By (
4.5
), we get
\sigma_{\varphi^{*}\Theta}=\sigma_{[\theta_{\infty}\wedge\kappa_{Z}^{q}]}
on
T^{o}
.
Since
T^{o}
is Stein,
H^{q}(Z\setminus Z_{0},K_{Z}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))\cong\Gamma(T^{o},R^{q}\varpi_{*}K_{Z}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))

by the Leray spectral sequence.
Hence

(4.6)

\varphi^{*}\Theta|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}=[\theta_{\infty}\wedge\kappa_{Z}^{q}]|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}.

(We remark that (
4.6
) follows directly from the fact that
\kappa_{Z,k}
can be constructed in such a way that

[\kappa_{Z,k}]|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}=[\kappa_{Z}]|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}
.
Indeed,
\varphi^{*}\Theta=[\theta_{\infty}\wedge\kappa_{Z}^{q}]
. See Section
9.2
.)

Since
\varphi\colon Z\to Y
is a proper modification,
there exists
\theta\in H^{0}(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{n+1-q}(F^{*}\xi))
with
\varphi^{*}\theta=\theta_{\infty}
.
Since
\kappa_{Z}=\varphi^{*}\kappa_{Y}
, we get

\Theta|_{Y\setminus Y_{0}}=(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\varphi^{*}\Theta|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}=(\varphi^{-1})^{*}[\theta_{\infty}\wedge\kappa_{Z}^{q}]|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}=[\theta\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}]|_{Y\setminus Y_{0}}.

Since
\varpi=f\circ\varphi
and hence
(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\varpi^{*}=f^{*}
, we get
(f^{*}dt)\wedge\theta=0
by the relation

(\varpi^{*}dt)\wedge\theta_{\infty}=0
.
Since
(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\widetilde{F}^{*}=F^{*}
,
\theta|_{Y\setminus Y_{0}}=(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\theta_{\infty}
and

Y\setminus Y_{0}
is a dense open subset of
Y
, we get
D^{F^{*}\xi}\theta=0
by
D^{\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi}\theta_{\infty}=0
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

In Proposition
4.2
, the equality
\Theta=[\theta\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}]
holds. See Section
9.2
.

4.4.
Asymptotic expansion of the
L^{2}
-metric

By the
G
-equivariance of the vector bundle
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)
,
its direct summand
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)_{W}
is a
G
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundle on
T
.
Let
h_{W}^{q}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
be its rank. Shrinking
T
if necessary, we assume that
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)
is a free

{\mathcal{O}}_{T}
-module on
T
.
Let
\{\Theta_{1},\ldots,\Theta_{h_{W}^{q}}\}\subset\Gamma(T,R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)_{W})
be a basis of

R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)_{W}
as a free
{\mathcal{O}}_{T}
-module.
Shrinking
T
if necessary, we regard
\Theta_{\alpha}\in\Gamma(T,R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi))=H^{q}(Y,K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi))

via the isomorphism (
1.6
).
By Proposition
4.2
, there exist a holomorphic differential form

\theta_{\alpha}\in\Gamma(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{n-q+1}(F^{*}\xi))

and a holomorphic relative differential form
\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\in\Gamma(Y\setminus Y_{0},\Omega_{Y/T}^{n-q}(F^{*}\xi))

such that

(4.7)

\Theta_{\alpha}|_{Y\setminus Y_{0}}=[\theta_{\alpha}\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}]|_{Y\setminus Y_{0}}\in H^{q}(Y\setminus Y_{0},K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi)),

(4.8)

\theta_{\alpha}|_{Y\setminus Y_{0}}=\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\wedge f^{*}dt,

(4.9)

D^{F^{*}\xi,F^{*}h_{\xi}}\theta_{\alpha}=0.

For
t\in T^{o}
, set
\kappa_{Y_{t}}:=\kappa_{Y}|_{Y_{t}}
.
By Theorem
2.1
(3),
\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q}
is the harmonic representative of
the class
[\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}]|_{Y_{t}}
with respect to the metrics
\kappa_{Y_{t}}
,
h_{F^{*}\xi}|_{Y_{t}}
.

Let
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}\}
be a local holomorphic frame of
F^{*}\xi
and
let
\{e_{1}^{\lor},\ldots,e_{r}^{\lor}\}
be its dual frame of
F^{*}\xi^{\lor}
.
We can express locally
\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}=\sum_{i}\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha,i}\otimes e_{i}
and

h_{F^{*}\xi}=\sum_{i,j}h_{i\bar{j}}e_{i}^{\lor}\otimes\bar{e}_{j}^{\lor}
,
where the
\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha,i}
are local holomorphic sections of
\Omega_{Y/T}^{q}
and
the
h_{i\bar{j}}
are local smooth functions. We define a smooth relative
(q,q)
-form by

h_{F^{*}\xi}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}}_{\beta}):=\sum_{i,j}h_{i\bar{j}}\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha,i}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}}_{\beta,j}.

For any
t\in T^{o}
, we have

h_{F^{*}\xi}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}}_{\beta})|_{Y_{t}}=h_{F^{*}\xi}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\overline{{\widetilde{\theta}}}_{\beta}|_{Y_{t}})\in A^{q,q}(Y_{t})
.
Since
\Theta_{\alpha}\otimes(f^{*}dt)^{-1}\in H^{q}(Y,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))
and
K_{Y/T}|_{Y_{t}}\cong K_{Y_{t}}
in the canonical way,
its restriction
\Theta_{\alpha}\otimes(f^{*}dt)^{-1}|_{Y_{t}}
is viewed as an element of
H^{q}(Y_{t},K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))
.
By (
4.7
), (
4.8
), under this identification, we have

\Theta_{\alpha}\otimes(f^{*}dt)^{-1}|_{Y_{t}}=[\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q}].

We set

(4.10)

\widetilde{H}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(t):=\left(\Theta_{\alpha}\otimes(f^{*}dt)^{-1}|_{Y_{t}},\Theta_{\beta}\otimes(f^{*}dt)^{-1}|_{Y_{t}}\right)_{L^{2}}\qquad(t\in T^{o}).

Then
\widetilde{H}(t)=(\widetilde{H}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(t))
is a positive-definite
(h_{W}^{q},h_{W}^{q})
-Hermitian matrix.

Lemma 4.4
.

For any
t\in T^{o}
, the following identity holds:

\widetilde{H}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(t)=q!\int_{Y_{t}}i^{(n-q)^{2}}h_{F^{*}\xi}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}}_{\beta})|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q}.

Proof.

Let
\star_{t}
be the Hodge star operator with respect to
\kappa_{Y_{t}}
.
Since
\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q}
is the harmonic representative of the cohomology class

[\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}]|_{Y_{t}}
by Theorem
2.1
(3), we have

H_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(t)=\int_{Y_{t}}\langle\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q},\widetilde{\theta}_{\beta}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q}\rangle_{t}dv_{t}
with
dv_{t}=\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{n}/n!
.
Since
\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q}=(q!/(-i)^{(n-q)^{2}})(\star_{t}\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}})

(cf.
[
35
, Cor. 2.2 (1)]
), we get

\langle\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q},\widetilde{\theta}_{\beta}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q}\rangle_{t}=(q!)^{2}\langle\star_{t}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}),\star_{t}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\beta}|_{Y_{t}})\rangle_{t}=(q!)^{2}\langle\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}},\widetilde{\theta}_{\beta}|_{Y_{t}}\rangle_{t}.

Since

\langle\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}},\widetilde{\theta}_{\beta}|_{Y_{t}}\rangle_{t}dv_{t}=h_{F^{*}\xi}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\overline{\star}_{t}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\beta}|_{Y_{t}}))=C_{n,q}h_{F^{*}\xi}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}_{\beta}}|_{Y_{t}})\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q}
with
C_{n,q}=i^{(n-q)^{2}}/q!
, we get the result.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

As is mentioned after Theorem
2.1
,
H_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(t)
is independent of the choice of Kähler form
within the Kähler class
[\kappa_{Y_{t}}]
. Indeed, for a
(q,q-1)
-form
\chi
on
Y_{t}
, we have

h_{F^{*}\xi}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}_{\beta}}|_{Y_{t}})\wedge\bar{\partial}\chi=\bar{\partial}_{t}\{h_{F^{*}\xi}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}_{\beta}}|_{Y_{t}})\wedge\chi\}

by the equality
D^{F^{*}\xi}_{t}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}|_{Y_{t}})=0
derived from the equality
D^{F^{*}\xi,F^{*}h_{\xi}}\theta_{\alpha}=0

in Proposition
4.2
, where
D^{F^{*}\xi}_{t}
is the Chern connection of
(F^{*}\xi,F^{*}h_{\xi})|_{Y_{t}}
.

Proposition 4.6
.

There exist smooth functions
A_{\alpha\bar{\beta};m}(t)\in C^{\infty}(T)

(1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq h_{W}^{q}
,
0\leq m\leq n)
such that

(4.11)

\widetilde{H}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(t)=\sum_{0\leq m\leq n}A_{\alpha\bar{\beta};m}(t)\,(\log|t|^{-2})^{m}.

In particular, there exist smooth functions
a_{m}(t)\in C^{\infty}(T)

(0\leq m\leq nh_{W}^{q})
with

(4.12)

\det\widetilde{H}(t)=\sum_{0\leq m\leq nh_{W}^{q}}a_{m}(t)(\log|t|^{-2})^{m}.

Proof.

On
Y\setminus Y_{0}
, by (
4.8
), we have

(4.13)

f^{*}(i\,dt\wedge d\bar{t})\wedge\{i^{(n-q)^{2}}h_{F^{*}{\xi}}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}}_{\beta})\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}\}=i^{(n-q+1)^{2}}h_{F^{*}{\xi}}(\theta_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\theta}_{\beta})\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}.

Let
\{{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be a finite open covering of
Y
such that there is a system of coordinates

(z_{0},\ldots,z_{n})
on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
with
f|_{{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}}(z)=z_{0}\cdots z_{k}
.
Here
k
depends on
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
We may suppose that
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
is biholomorphic to a polydisc of dimension
n+1
for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
Let us write
u=(u_{0},\ldots,u_{k})
and
v=(v_{k+1},\ldots,v_{n})
.
Then

Y_{t}\cap V_{\lambda}=W_{t}\times\varDelta^{n-k},\qquad W_{t}:=\{u\in\varDelta^{k+1};\,u_{0}\cdots u_{k}=t\}.

Let
\{\varrho_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be a partition of unity subject to
\{{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.
We set
du:=du_{0}\wedge\cdots\wedge du_{k}
and
dv:=dv_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dv_{n}
.
Since
\theta_{\alpha}
and
\theta_{\beta}
are holomorphic
n-q+1
-forms on
Y
and
\kappa_{Y}\in A^{1,1}(Y)
,
there exists
B_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}^{(\lambda)}(u,v)\in C^{\infty}_{0}({\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})
such that

(4.14)

i^{(n-q+1)^{2}}\varrho_{\lambda}(z)h_{F^{*}{\xi}}(\theta_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\theta}_{\beta})\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}|_{{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}}=B_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}^{(\lambda)}(u,v)\,du\wedge\overline{du}\wedge dv\wedge\overline{dv}.

Define
C_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}^{(\lambda)}(u)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\varDelta^{k+1})
by

C_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}^{(\lambda)}(u):=\int_{\varDelta^{n-k}}B_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}^{(\lambda)}(u,v)\,dv\wedge\overline{dv}
.
By Takayama
[
45
, Proof of Prop. 6.1]
applied to the fiber integral

\int_{W_{t}}C_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}^{(\lambda)}(u)du\wedge\overline{du}
, there exist smooth functions

A_{\alpha\bar{\beta};m}^{(\lambda)}(t)\in C^{\infty}(T)

(0\leq m\leq k)
such that

(4.15)

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{t}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}}B_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}^{(\lambda)}(u,v)du\wedge d\overline{u}\wedge dv\wedge d\overline{v}

\displaystyle=\int_{W_{t}}C_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}^{(\lambda)}(u)du\wedge\overline{du}

\displaystyle=\{\sum_{0\leq m\leq k}A_{\alpha\bar{\beta};m}^{(\lambda)}(t)(\log|t|)^{m}\}\,i\,dt\wedge d\overline{t}.

By (
4.13
), (
4.14
), (
4.15
), as functions on
T^{o}
, we have

(4.16)

\int_{Y_{t}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}}i^{(n-q)^{2}}\,\varrho_{\lambda}(z)h_{F^{*}{\xi}}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}}_{\beta})|_{Y_{t}}\wedge\kappa_{Y_{t}}^{q}=\sum_{0\leq m\leq k}A_{\alpha\bar{\beta};m}^{(\lambda)}(t)(\log|t|^{2})^{m}.

Since

\widetilde{H}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}=\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}i^{(n-q)^{2}}f_{*}(\varrho_{\lambda}\,h_{F^{*}{\xi}}(\widetilde{\theta}_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\theta}}_{\beta})\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q})
,
the result follows from (
4.16
). This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

For
q=0
, Proposition
4.6
is a special case of
[
45
, Prop. 6.1]
.
In fact, for
q=0
, this proposition holds even if
(\xi,h_{\xi})
is not Nakano semi-positive.

4.5.
Non-degeneracy of the
L^{2}
-metric

To prove the non-vanishing of
a_{m}(0)
for some
0\leq m\leq nh_{W}^{q}
in Proposition
4.6
,
we need the following:

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
M
be a compact complex manifold and let
\omega
,
\omega^{\prime}
be Kähler metrics on
M
.
Let
(E,h)
be a holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle on
M
.
Let
h_{L^{2},\omega,h}
(resp.
h_{L^{2},\omega^{\prime},h}
) be the
L^{2}
-metric on
H^{q}(M,K_{M}(E))
with respect to
the metrics
\omega
,
h
(resp.
\omega^{\prime}
,
h
). If
\omega\leq\omega^{\prime}
as Hermitian forms on
T^{(1,0)}M
, then

h_{L^{2},\omega^{\prime},h}\leq h_{L^{2},\omega,h}.

Proof.

Let
\theta\in H^{q}(M,K_{M}(E))
. Let
{\mathcal{H}}_{\omega,h}(\theta)
(resp.
{\mathcal{H}}_{\omega^{\prime},h}(\theta)
)
be the harmonic representative of
\theta
with respect to the metrics
\omega
,
h
(resp.
\omega^{\prime}
,
h
).
Let
dv_{\omega}
and
dv_{\omega^{\prime}}
be the volume forms of
\omega
and
\omega^{\prime}
, respectively. Then we have

\int_{M}|{\mathcal{H}}_{\omega,h}(\theta)|_{\omega,h}^{2}dv_{\omega}\geq\int_{M}|{\mathcal{H}}_{\omega,h}(\theta)|_{\omega^{\prime},h}^{2}dv_{\omega^{\prime}}\geq\int_{M}|{\mathcal{H}}_{\omega^{\prime},h}(\theta)|_{\omega^{\prime},h}^{2}dv_{\omega^{\prime}},

where the first inequality follows from
[
16
, Chap.VIII, Lemma 6.3]
and the second inequality follows from the fact
that the harmonic form has the minimal
L^{2}
-norm among all
\bar{\partial}
-closed differential forms in the given cohomology class.
The result follows from this inequality.
∎

Let
\kappa^{\prime}_{Y}
be a Kähler form on
Y
. Multiplying a positive constant and shrinking
T
if necessary, we may assume

\kappa_{Y}\leq\kappa^{\prime}_{Y}
. Let
(\cdot,\cdot)_{Y_{t},L^{2}}
(resp.
(\cdot,\cdot)^{\prime}_{Y_{t},L^{2}}
) be the
L^{2}
-metric on

H^{q}(Y_{t}K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))
with respect to
\kappa_{Y}|_{Y_{t}}
,
F^{*}h_{\xi}|_{Y_{t}}

(resp.
\kappa^{\prime}_{Y}|_{Y_{t}}
,
F^{*}h_{\xi}|_{Y_{t}}
).
The corresponding
L^{2}
-norms are denoted by
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2},Y_{t}}
and
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{L^{2},Y_{t}}
, respectively.
Since
\kappa^{\prime}_{Y}
is a non-degenerate Kähler form on
Y
and
Y_{0}
is a reduced normal crossing divisor on
Y
,
the non-degeneracy result of Mourougane-Takayama
[
36
, Lemmas 4.7, 4.8]
is applicable to
the
L^{2}
-metric on
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)
induced from
(\cdot,\cdot)^{\prime}_{L^{2},t}
.

Proposition 4.9
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
t\in T^{o}
,

(4.17)

\widetilde{H}(t)\geq C\,I_{h_{W}^{q}}

as positive definite Hermitian matrices.
In particular,
\det\widetilde{H}(t)\geq C^{h_{W}^{q}}
on
T^{o}
.

Proof.

\{\Theta_{1}\otimes(f^{*}dt)^{-1},\ldots,\Theta_{h_{W}^{q}}\otimes(f^{*}dt)^{-1}\}
is a basis of the free
{\mathcal{O}}_{T}
-module

R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)_{W}
.
Since
Y_{0}
is a reduced normal crossing divisor on
Y
and
\kappa^{\prime}_{Y}
is a non-degenerate Kähler from on
Y
,
by
[
36
, Lemmas 4.7 and 4.8]
applied to
f\colon Y\to T
and
F^{*}\xi
with respect to the metrics

\kappa^{\prime}_{Y}
,
F^{*}h_{\xi}
, there is a constant
C_{0}>0
such that
for all
u=(u_{\alpha})\in{\mathbf{C}}^{h_{W}^{q}}
and
t\in T^{o}
,

(4.18)

\|\sum_{\alpha}u_{\alpha}\Theta_{\alpha}\otimes(f^{*}dt)^{-1}|_{Y_{t}}\|^{\prime}_{L^{2},Y_{t}}\geq C_{0}\,|u|.

(Note that since
Y_{0}
is a reduced normal crossing divisor, we can take
\tau^{\circ}=\nu=\mu={\rm id}_{X}
and
\tau={\rm id}_{Y}

in
[
36
, Sect. 3.1]
and hence
X^{\prime\prime}=X
and
Y^{\prime}=Y
in
[
36
, Sect. 4.3]
.)
Since
\kappa_{Y}|_{Y_{t}}\leq\kappa^{\prime}_{Y}|_{Y_{t}}
,
we deduce from Lemma
4.8
that for all
\sigma\in H^{q}(Y_{t},K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))
,

(4.19)

\|\sigma\|_{L^{2},Y_{t}}\geq\|\sigma\|^{\prime}_{L^{2},Y_{t}}.

By (
4.18
), (
4.19
), setting
\sigma=\sum_{\alpha}u_{\alpha}\Theta_{\alpha}\otimes(f^{*}dt)^{-1}
,
we get (
4.17
) with
C=C_{0}^{2}
.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

Suppose that
(\xi,h_{\xi})|_{X}
is
Nakano positive
. By the Nakano vanishing theorem
[
16
, Cor. 7.5]
,

R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)=0
for
q>0
.
Let
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
be a Hermitian metric on
\xi
such that
(\xi,h^{\prime}_{\xi})|_{X}
is
not
necessarily Nakano semi-positive.
Even in this setting, Propositions
4.2
,
4.6
,
4.9

for
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
remain valid.
For Proposition
4.2
, this is obvious. Since the proof of Proposition
4.6
works
if Proposition
4.2
holds, Proposition
4.6
remains valid for
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
.
Since there exists a constant
C>0
such that
C^{-1}h_{\xi}\leq h^{\prime}_{\xi}\leq Ch_{\xi}
on
X
,
Proposition
4.9
for
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
follows from that for
h_{\xi}
.

4.6.
Singularity of the
L^{2}
-metric on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)

In this subsection, we describe the structure of the singularity of the
L^{2}
-metric on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
.
By Lemma
2.2
or
[
46
, Th. 6.5 (i)]
,
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
is a locally free sheaf on
S
.

Proposition 4.11
.

There is a
G
-equivariant injective homomorphism of sheaves

\varphi\colon R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)\hookrightarrow\mu^{*}R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)

with the following properties:

(1)

\mu^{*}R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)/\varphi(R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))
is a torsion sheaf on
T
supported at
0
.

(2)

\varphi^{*}\mu^{*}h_{R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)}=h_{R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)}
.
Here
h_{R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)}
(resp.
h_{R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)}
) is the
L^{2}
-metric on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)

(resp.
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)
) with respect to
\kappa_{X}
,
h_{\xi}
(resp.
\kappa_{Y}
,
F^{*}h_{\xi}
).

Proof.

Except for the
G
-equivariance of
\varphi
, the assertions were proved in
[
36
, Lemmas 3.3 and 4.2]
.
Since
\varphi|_{T^{o}}
is
G
-equivariant and
\varphi
is defined on
T
,
\varphi
is
G
-equivariant on
T
.
∎

For
q\geq 0
and
W\in\widehat{G}
, we set

(4.20)

\delta_{W}^{q}:=\frac{1}{\deg\mu}\dim_{\mathbf{C}}\left(\frac{\mu^{*}R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)_{W}}{R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)_{W}}\right)_{0}\in{\mathbf{Q}}_{\geq 0}.

We are now in a position to state the main theorem of this section, which extends the structure theorem of the singularity of
the
L^{2}
-metrics on the direct image sheaves of the relative canonical bundles
[
18
, Prop. 2.10]

to the case of those twisted by Nakano semi-positive vector bundles. Write
M_{r}({\mathbf{C}})
for the complex
(r,r)
-matrices.
Let
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty}_{0}(T)
be the smooth functions on
T
vanishing at
t=0
.

Theorem 4.12
.

By choosing suitable bases
\{{\mathbf{e}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{e}}_{h_{W}^{q}}\}
of
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)_{W}
and

\{\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{h_{W}^{q}}\}
of
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)_{W}
,
there exist integers
e^{q}_{1},\ldots,e^{q}_{h_{W}^{q}}\geq 0
with the following properties:

(1)

The
(h_{W}^{q},h_{W}^{q})
-Hermitian matrices
H(s):=(H_{\alpha\beta}(s))
,

H_{\alpha\beta}(s):=({\mathbf{e}}_{\alpha},{\mathbf{e}}_{\beta})_{L^{2},X_{s}}
, and

\widetilde{H}(t)=(\widetilde{H}_{\alpha\beta}(t))
,

\widetilde{H}_{\alpha\beta}(t):=(\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{\beta})_{L^{2},Y_{t}}
,
are related via the following identity

(4.21)

H(\mu(t))=D(t)\cdot\widetilde{H}(t)\cdot\overline{D(t)},\qquad D(t)={\rm diag}(t^{-e^{q}_{1}},\ldots,t^{-e^{q}_{h_{W}^{q}}}).

Moreover,
\widetilde{H}(t)
admits an asymptotic expansion of Barlet-Takayama type

(4.22)

\widetilde{H}(t)\equiv\sum_{0\leq m\leq n}(\log|t|^{-2})^{m}A_{m}\mod\bigoplus_{0\leq k\leq n}(\log|t|^{-2})^{k}{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty}_{0}(T)\otimes M_{h_{W}^{q}}({\mathbf{C}})

with constant
(h_{W}^{q},h_{W}^{q})
-Hermitian matrices
A_{m}

(1\leq m\leq n)
.
In particular, there exist constants
a_{m}^{q}\in{\mathbf{R}}

(1\leq m\leq nh_{W}^{q})
such that

(4.23)

\det\widetilde{H}(t)=\sum_{0\leq m\leq nh_{W}^{q}}a_{m}^{q}(\log|t|^{-2})^{m}\mod\bigoplus_{0\leq k\leq nh_{W}^{q}}(\log|t|^{-2})^{k}{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty}_{0}(T).

(2)

There exists a constant
C>0
such that
\widetilde{H}(t)\geq C\,I_{h_{W}^{q}}
for all
t\in T^{o}
as positive definite Hermitian matrices.
Especially,
a_{m}^{q}\not=0
for some
1\leq m\leq nh_{W}^{q}
.

(3)

There exist real-valued smooth functions
\psi_{j,k}^{q}(s)
on
S
such that

(4.24)

\|{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge{\mathbf{e}}_{h_{W}^{q}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=|s|^{-2\delta_{W}^{q}}\sum_{0\leq m\leq nh_{W}^{q}}\{a_{m}^{q}+\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}|s|^{\frac{2j}{d}}\psi_{j,m}^{q}(s)\}(\log|s|^{-2})^{m}.

Let

(4.25)

\varrho_{W}^{q}:=\max\{0\leq m\leq nh_{W}^{q};\,a_{m}^{q}\not=0\}.

Then the following equality holds as
s\to 0
:

(4.26)

\log\|{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge{\mathbf{e}}_{h_{W}^{q}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-\delta_{W}^{q}\,\log|s|^{2}+\varrho_{W}^{q}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c_{W}^{q}+O\left(1/\log|s|^{-1}\right).

(4)

The rational numbers
e^{q}_{\alpha}/\deg\mu
(
1\leq\alpha\leq h_{W}^{q}
) are independent of the choice of semi-stable reduction.

Proof.

By choosing bases
\{{\mathbf{e}}_{\alpha}\}
,
\{\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{\alpha}\}
suitably,
it follows from Proposition
4.11
(1) that there exist integers

e_{\alpha}=e^{q}_{\alpha}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
(
1\leq\alpha\leq h_{W}^{q}
) satisfying

(4.27)

\mu^{*}{\mathbf{e}}_{\alpha}=t^{-e^{q}_{\alpha}}\varphi(\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{\alpha})\qquad(t\in T).

By (
4.27
) and Proposition
4.11
(2), we get

H_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(\mu(t))=t^{-e_{\alpha}}\bar{t}^{-e_{\beta}}\widetilde{H}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(t)
.
This proves (
4.21
). The expansions (
4.22
), (
4.23
) follow from
(
4.11
), (
4.12
), respectively. This proves (1).
The inequality
\widetilde{H}(t)\geq CI_{h_{W}^{q}}
follows from Proposition
4.9
.
By this inequality,
a_{m}^{q}\not=0
for some
0\leq m\leq nh_{W}^{q}
.
This proves (2). Since

(4.28)

\frac{1}{\deg\mu}\sum_{\alpha}e^{q}_{\alpha}=\delta_{W}^{q}

by (
4.20
), (
4.27
), we deduce from (
4.21
), (
4.28
) that

(4.29)

\det H(\mu(t))=|t|^{-2\deg\mu\cdot\delta_{W}^{q}}\det\widetilde{H}(t).

Since
|t|=|s|^{1/\deg\mu}
and
\|{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge{\mathbf{e}}_{h_{W}^{q}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\det H(s)
,
(
4.24
) follows from (
4.12
), (
4.23
), (
4.29
)
and Lemma
4.14
below.
We deduce (
4.26
) from (
4.24
). This proves (3).
Since any two semi-stable reductions of
\pi\colon X\to S
are dominated by the third one and since the integers
e^{q}_{\alpha}

(
1\leq\alpha\leq h_{W}^{q}
) depend only on the semi-stable reduction, we get (4). This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

The proof of Theorem
4.12
relies on Propositions
4.2
,
4.6

and
4.9
.
In the previous version
[
52
]
, the proof of
[
52
, Lemma 6.3]

(corresponding to Proposition
4.6
here) relied on a theorem of Barlet
[
2
, Th. 4 bis]
.
Since that theorem can no longer be relied upon in its original form, as shown by Takayama
[
44
, Th. 7.1.3, Remark 7.1.4]
,
we now provide an alternative proof based on
[
45
, Prop. 6.1]
(the calculations in
[
2
, p.140 Cas 1]
).
Furthermore, the proof of
[
52
, Lemma 6.4]
(now Proposition
4.9
) previously utilized
a non-degeneracy result from Mourougane-Takayama
[
36
, Lemma 4.8]
for the family
\varpi\colon Z\to T
.
However, as the Kähler form
\kappa_{Z}
on
Z
may degenerate on certain non-
\widetilde{\rho}
-exceptional components of
Z_{0}
,

[
36
, Lemma 4.1]
does not necessarily hold for
\kappa_{Z}
.
Consequently,
[
36
, Lemma 4.8]
cannot be directly applied to the pair
\varpi\colon Z\to T
and
\kappa_{Z}
.
This difficulty is circumvented by employing Lemma
4.8
.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\varphi(t)\in C^{\infty}(T)
. Set
s=t^{d}
. If
\varphi(\zeta t)=\varphi(t)
,
\zeta=e^{2\pi i/d}
,
then there exist
\psi_{j}(s)\in C^{\infty}(S)

(0\leq j\leq d-1)
such that
\varphi(t)=\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}|s|^{\frac{2j}{d}}\psi_{j}(s)
.

Proof.

Since the map
C^{\infty}(T)\to{\mathbf{C}}[[t,\bar{t}]]
assigning
F(t)\in C^{\infty}(T)
to its Taylor series at the origin is surjective
by Borel,
there exists
\psi_{j}(s)\in C^{\infty}(S)

(0\leq j\leq d-1)
such that
\chi(t):=\varphi(t)-\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}|s|^{\frac{2j}{d}}\psi_{j}(s)

has the vanishing Taylor series at
t=0
. Since
\chi(\zeta t)=\chi(t)
,
\chi(s^{\frac{1}{d}})
is well-defined and lies in

C^{0}(S)\cap C^{\infty}(S^{o})
. Write
s=x+iy=r(\cos\theta+\sqrt{-1}\sin\theta)
.
Since
\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{j}\chi(s^{1/d})
for
s\not=0
is a linear combination of the various derivatives of
\chi(t)

up to the order
i+j
and some polynomials in the variables
r^{-1}
,
r^{1/d}
,
\cos(\theta/d)
,
\sin(\theta/d)
,
the condition that
\chi(t)
has the vanishing Taylor series at
t=0
implies that

\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{j}\chi(s^{1/d})\to 0
as
s\to 0
for all
i,j\geq 0
. Hence
\chi(s^{1/d})\in C^{\infty}(S)
.
Replacing
\psi_{0}(s)
with
\psi_{0}(s)+\chi(s^{1/d})
, we get the result.
∎

After
[
18
, Def. 2.5]
and Theorem
4.12
, we make the following:

Definition 4.15
.

The rational numbers
\epsilon^{q}_{\alpha}:=e^{q}_{\alpha}/\deg\mu
(
1\leq\alpha\leq h_{W}^{q}
) are called
the elementary exponents of the vector bundle
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)_{W}
.
The elementary exponents of
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
are defined to be those for the trivial group
G=\{1\}
.

By (
4.28
), we have

(4.30)

\delta_{W}^{q}=\sum_{\alpha}\epsilon^{q}_{\alpha}.

We define

(4.31)

\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g):=\frac{1}{\deg\mu}\sum_{q\geq 0}(-1)^{q}{\rm Tr}[g|_{(\mu^{*}R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)/R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))_{0}}].

By (
4.27
), (
4.30
), we have

(4.32)

\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)=\sum_{W\in\widehat{G}}\sum_{q\geq 0}(-1)^{q}\frac{\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}\delta_{W}^{q}=\sum_{W\in\widehat{G}}\sum_{q\geq 0}\sum_{\alpha}(-1)^{q}\frac{\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}\epsilon_{\alpha}^{q}.

We often write
\delta_{g}
for
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)
when there is no possibility of confusion.
Similarly, we write
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
for
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(1)
. In Corollary
7.8
below,
we will give an expression of
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
in terms of various characteristic classes associated to
the semi-stable reduction
f\colon Y\to T
.

Remark 4.16
.

If
(\xi,h_{\xi})|_{X}
is Nakano positive, then Theorem
4.12
holds for any Hermitian metric
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
on
\xi
,
whose curvature is not necessarily semi-positive. Indeed, by Remark
4.10
,
the proof of Theorem
4.12
works in this case.

After Theorem
4.12
and Remark
4.16
, we ask the following:

Question 4.17
.

In the situation of Theorem
4.12
, let
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
be any Hermitian metric on
\xi
,
whose curvature form is not necessarily semi-positive definite.
Then does Theorem
4.12
hold for
(\xi,h^{\prime}_{\xi})|_{X}
? Remark
4.16
implies that this is the case
if
(\xi,h_{\xi})|_{X}
is Nakano positive.

If we drop the semi-positivity of the curvature of the Hermitian metric on
\xi
,
we still have the following weak form of Theorem
4.12
(3).

Theorem 4.18
.

Let
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
be another Hermitian metric on
\xi
such that
(\xi,h^{\prime}_{\xi})|_{X}
is not necessarily Nakano semi-positive.
Then, as
s\to 0
,

\log\|\sigma_{W}^{q}(s)\|_{L^{2},h^{\prime}_{\xi}}^{2}=-\delta_{W}^{q}\,\log|s|^{2}+\varrho_{W}^{q}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+O\left(1\right),

where
\delta_{W}^{q}
and
\varrho_{W}^{q}
are the same constants as in Theorem
4.12
(3).

Proof.

We write
h^{q}_{L^{2}}
and
h^{\prime q}_{L^{2}}
for the
L^{2}
-metrics on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
with respect to

h_{\xi}
and
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
, respectively. For a cohomology class
\theta\in H^{q}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))
,
we write
{\mathcal{H}}(\theta)
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\theta)
for the harmonic representatives of
\theta
with respect to

h_{\xi}
and
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
, respectively. Let
dv_{s}
be the volume form of
h_{X_{s}}
.
Let
C>0
be a constant such that
C^{-1}h^{\prime}_{\xi}\leq h_{\xi}\leq Ch^{\prime}_{\xi}
as Hermitian metrics on
\xi
. Then

\int_{X_{s}}\langle{\mathcal{H}}(\theta),{\mathcal{H}}(\theta)\rangle_{h_{\xi}}dv_{s}\leq\int_{X_{s}}\langle{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\theta),{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle_{h_{\xi}}dv_{s}\leq C\int_{X_{s}}\langle{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\theta),{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle_{h^{\prime}_{\xi}}dv_{s},

where the first inequality follows from the fact that the harmonic form minimizes the
L^{2}
-norm in the given Dolbeault cohomology class
and the second inequality follows from the inequality
h_{\xi}\leq Ch^{\prime}_{\xi}
. From this inequality, we obtain the inequality of
Hermitian metrics
h^{q}_{L^{2}}\leq Ch^{\prime q}_{L^{2}}
on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
.
Similarly, we get
C^{-1}h^{\prime q}_{L^{2}}\leq h^{q}_{L^{2}}
.
By these inequalities of the
L^{2}
-metrics, we obtain
\log(\|\cdot\|_{L^{2},h^{\prime}_{\xi}}/\|\cdot\|_{L^{2},h_{\xi}})=O(1)
as
s\to 0
.
This, together with Theorem
4.12
(3), yields the result.
∎

5.
The singularity of analytic torsion near the boundary

In this section, we prove the existence of the asymptotic expansion of the logarithm of (equivariant) analytic torsion
viewed as a function on the punctured disc, whose leading term is a logarithmic singularity and whose subdominant term is
a
\log\log
-type singularity. We determine the coefficient of the logarithmic singularity
when
X_{0}
has only canonical singularities or
\xi
is the trivial Hermitian line bundle.

5.1.
The existence of asymptotic expansion

Recall that
\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)
and
\varrho^{q}_{W}
were defined by (
3.8
) and (
4.25
), respectively.
Define

(5.1)

\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)=\kappa_{g}:=\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)+\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g),

(5.2)

\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)=\varrho_{g}:=\sum_{q\geq 0,\,W\in\widehat{G}}(-1)^{q}\chi_{W}(g)\varrho_{W}^{q}/\dim W.

When
g=1
, we write
\kappa_{K_{X/S}(\xi)}
and
\varrho_{K_{X/S}(\xi)}
in place of
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(1)
and

\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(1)
, respectively.

Theorem 5.1
.

If
(\xi,h_{\xi})
is Nakano semi-positive on
X=\pi^{-1}(S)
, then the equivariant analytic torsion
\tau_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)

is expressed as follows on
S^{o}
:

(5.3)

\displaystyle\log\tau_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)=\kappa_{g}\log|s|^{2}+c_{g}+\sum_{0\leq m\leq n}\sum_{i\in I}|s|^{2r_{i}}(\log|s|^{-2})^{m}\phi_{i,m,g}(s)

\displaystyle-\sum_{q\geq 0,W\in\widehat{G}}\frac{(-1)^{q}\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}\log\left(\sum_{0\leq k\leq nh_{W}^{q}}\{a_{k,W}^{q}+\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}|s|^{\frac{2j}{d}}\psi_{j,k,W}^{q}(s)\}(\log|s|^{-2})^{k}\right),

where
c_{g}\in{\mathbf{C}}
,
\{r_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subset{\mathbf{Q}}\cap(0,1]
is a finite set of positive rational numbers,

(a_{0,W}^{q},\ldots,a_{nh_{W}^{q},W}^{q})\not=(0,\ldots,0)
is the non-zero real constant vector
appearing in (
4.24
), the
\phi_{i,m,W}(s)
are complex-valued smooth functions on
S
,
and the
\psi_{j,k,W}^{q}(s)
are real-valued smooth functions on
S
.
In particular, there exists a constant
\gamma_{g}\in{\mathbf{C}}
such that as
s\to 0

(5.4)

\log\tau_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)=\kappa_{g}\log|s|^{2}-\varrho_{g}\,\log\log(|s|^{-2})+\gamma_{g}+O\left(1/\log|s|^{-1}\right).

Proof.

Let
\sigma:=(\sigma_{W})_{W\in\widehat{G}}
be an admissible section of

\lambda_{G}(K_{X/S}(\xi))=\lambda_{G}(K_{{\mathcal{X}}/C}(\xi))|_{S}
.
By Theorem
3.4
, there exist a finite set of rational numbers
\{r_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subset{\mathbf{Q}}\cap(0,1]
,
complex-valued smooth functions
\phi_{i,m,g}(s)
on
S
and a constant
c_{g}\in{\mathbf{C}}
such that

(5.5)

\displaystyle\log\|\sigma(s)\|_{\lambda_{G}(K_{{\mathcal{X}}/C}(\xi)),Q}^{2}(g)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)\,\log|s|^{2}+c_{g}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq m\leq n}\sum_{i\in I}|s|^{2r_{i}}(\log|s|^{-2})^{m}\phi_{i,m,g}(s)

as functions on
S^{o}
. By Theorem
4.12
(2), we can express

(5.6)

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{\|\sigma(s)\|_{\lambda_{G}(K_{{\mathcal{X}}/C}(\xi)),Q}^{2}(g)}{\tau_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)}\right)=\sum_{q\geq 0,\,W\in\widehat{G}}\frac{(-1)^{q}\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}\log\|\sigma^{q}_{W}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q\geq 0,\,W\in\widehat{G}}\frac{(-1)^{q}\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}\log\left(\sum_{0\leq k\leq nh_{W}^{q}}\{a_{k,W}^{q}+\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}|s|^{\frac{2j}{d}}\psi_{j,k,W}^{q}(s)\}(\log|s|^{-2})^{k}\right).

The expansion (
5.3
) follows from (
5.5
) and (
5.6
).
By (
4.26
), (
4.32
), (
5.2
), we find

(5.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{q\geq 0,\,W\in\widehat{G}}\frac{(-1)^{q}\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}\log\|\sigma^{q}_{W}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q,\,W}(-1)^{q}\frac{\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}\left\{-\delta_{W}^{q}\,\log|s|^{2}+\varrho_{W}^{q}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c_{W}^{q}+O\left(1/\log|s|^{-1}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=-\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)\log|s|^{2}+\varrho_{g}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c^{\prime}_{g}+O\left(1/\log|s|^{-1}\right),

where we set
c^{\prime}_{g}:=\sum_{q\geq 0,\,W\in\widehat{G}}(-1)^{q}\chi_{W}(g)c_{W}^{q}/\dim W
.
Then (
5.4
) is derived from (
5.5
), (
5.7
) with
\gamma_{g}:=c_{g}-c^{\prime}_{g}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.2
.

As
s\to 0
, the following equality holds:

\partial_{s\bar{s}}\log\tau(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)=\frac{\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)}{|s|^{2}(\log|s|)^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{|s|^{2}(\log|s|)^{3}}\right).

Proof.

The result follows from (
5.3
) and Lemma
8.2
below.
∎

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
(E,h_{E})
be a holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle on
{\mathcal{X}}
.
If
(E,h_{E})
is semi-negative in the dual Nakano sense on
X
, then as
s\to 0
,

(-1)^{n+1}\log\tau_{G}(X_{s},E_{s})(g)=\kappa_{g}\log|s|^{2}-\varrho_{g}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c_{g}+O\left(1/\log|s|^{-1}\right).

Here
\kappa_{g},\varrho_{g},c_{g}
are the constants given by (
5.1
), (
5.2
) with
\xi=E^{\lor}
.

Proof.

Let
\square_{E_{s}}^{p,q}
be the Laplacian acting on
A^{p,q}(X_{s},E_{s})
and let

\bar{\star}_{E_{s}}\colon A^{p,q}(X_{s},E_{s})\to A^{n-p,n-q}(X_{s},E_{s}^{\lor})
be the Hodge star operator (cf.
[
48
, p.166]
).
Since

\bar{\star}_{E_{s}}\square_{E_{s}}^{0,q}\bar{\star}_{E_{s}}^{-1}=\square_{E_{s}^{\lor}}^{n,n-q}=\square_{K_{X_{s}}(E_{s}^{\lor})}^{0,n-q}

by
[
48
, Chap. V Prop. 2.4 (b)]
, we have

(5.8)

\log\tau_{G}(X_{s},E_{s})(g)=(-1)^{n+1}\log\tau_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(E_{s}^{\lor}))(g).

Since
(E^{\lor},h_{E^{\lor}})
is Nakano semi-positive on
X
, the result follows from Theorem
5.1
and (
5.8
).
∎

Corollary 5.4
.

If
(\xi,h_{\xi})
is Nakano semi-positive on
X
and
X_{0}
is a reduced normal crossing divisor of
X
, then as
s\to 0

\log\tau_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)=\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)\log|s|^{2}-\varrho_{g}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c_{g}+O\left(1/\log|s|^{-1}\right).

Proof.

We have
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)=0
in Theorem
5.1
, since
\pi\colon X\to S
is a semi-stable degeneration.
The result follows from Theorem
5.1
.
∎

Remark 5.5
.

Suppose that
(\xi,h_{\xi})|_{X}
is Nakano positive. Let
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
be an arbitrary Hermitian metric on
\xi
.
In particular,
(\xi,h^{\prime}_{\xi})
is not necessarily Nakano semi-positive on
X
.
Even in this case, the asymptotic expansion (
5.4
) still holds. In fact, since Theorem
4.12
holds
in this case by Remark
4.16
, the proof of Theorem
5.1
works.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
be another Hermitian metric on
\xi
such that
(\xi,h^{\prime}_{\xi})
is not necessarily Nakano semi-positive.
Let
\tau^{\prime}_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)
be the equivariant analytic torsion of
\xi_{s}
with respect to the metrics

\kappa_{X_{s}}
,
h^{\prime}_{\xi}
. Then, as
s\to 0
,

\log\tau^{\prime}_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)=\kappa_{g}\log|s|^{2}-\varrho_{g}\,\log\log(|s|^{-2})+O\left(1\right).

Proof.

By Theorem
4.18
, we have the following weak form of (
5.7
):

\sum_{q\geq 0,\,W\in\widehat{G}}\frac{(-1)^{q}\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}\log\|\sigma^{q}_{W}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)\log|s|^{2}+\varrho_{g}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+O\left(1\right).

Substituting this into the first equality of (
5.6
) and using (
5.5
), we get the result.
∎

5.2.
Hodge theory and the singularity of analytic torsion

Throughout this subsection, we assume that
\xi|_{X}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is the trivial line bundle and that
h_{\xi}|_{X}
is the trivial metric.
Then the singularity of the
L^{2}
-metric on
\lambda(K_{X/S})
was determined by
Eriksson-Freixas i Montplet-Mourougane
[
18
, Prop. 2.10]
.
When
X_{0}
has at most isolated singularities, the singularity of the analytic torsion
\tau(X_{s},{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{s}})
was determined by
Eriksson-Freixas i Montplet
[
19
, Cor. 3.2]
.
In this subsection, we recall the formula for
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}}
.
In Section
7
, these results will be used to express
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}

when
\xi
is topologically trivial near
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
.

5.2.1.
Hodge theory and the singularity of the
L^{2}
-metrics

To describe the structure of the singularity of the
L^{2}
-metric on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}
(
[
18
, Prop. 2.10]
),
we introduce some notation.
Since
S\cong\varDelta
is endowed with the coordinate
s
, fixing a reference point of
S^{o}
and the corresponding fiber
X_{\infty}
,
we have the limiting mixed Hodge structure on
H^{r}(X_{\infty})
with limiting Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}_{\infty}H^{r}(X_{\infty})

(
[
38
]
,
[
41
]
).
Let
M\in{\rm GL}(H(X_{\infty}))
be the monodromy for the family
\pi^{o}\colon X^{o}\to S^{o}
.
Let
M_{s}
be the semi-simple part of
M
. Then
M_{s}
preserves
F^{k}_{\infty}H^{r}(X_{\infty})
(
[
41
, Th. 2.13]
).
Since the
G
-action on
R^{r}\pi_{*}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{o}}
is parallel with respect to the Gauss-Manin connection,

G
acts on the multi-valued flat sections of
R^{r}\pi_{*}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{o}}
over
S^{o}

and the
G
-action on the flat sections of
R^{r}\pi_{*}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{o}}
commutes with
M
, hence
M_{s}
.
Then
G
acts on the (upper) canonical extension
{\mathcal{H}}^{r}\cong H^{r}(X_{\infty})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
of

R^{r}\pi_{*}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{o}}
.
Since the
G
-action on
R^{r}\pi_{*}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{o}}
preserves the Hodge bundles

F^{k}(R^{r}\pi_{*}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{o}})

(k\geq 0)
,
G
acts on the
F^{k}{\mathcal{H}}^{r}

(k\geq 0)
.
In particular,
G
acts on
F^{k}_{\infty}H^{r}(X_{\infty})
.
Since the
M_{s}
-action commutes with the
G
-action,
M_{s}
acts on

F^{k}_{\infty}H^{r}(X_{\infty})_{W}:={\rm Hom}_{G}(W,F^{k}_{\infty}H^{r}(X_{\infty}))\otimes W

for all
W\in\widehat{G}
, where
M_{s}
acts trivially on
W
, and the
M_{s}
-action on
F^{k}_{\infty}H^{r}(X_{\infty})

is identified with the direct sum of these
M_{s}
-actions on
F^{k}_{\infty}H^{r}(X_{\infty})_{W}
.
Let
\log z
be the logarithm whose imaginary part lies in the interval
[0,2\pi)
and
let
\log M_{s}
be the corresponding logarithm of
M_{s}
. Then
\log M_{s}
acts on
F^{k}H^{r}(X_{\infty})_{W}
.

Proposition 5.7
.

The elementary exponents of
(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S})_{W}
are the eigenvalues of
\frac{1}{2\pi i}\log M_{s}

acting on
F^{n}_{\infty}H^{n+q}(X_{\infty})_{W}
. In particular,

\delta_{W}^{q}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}{\rm Tr}\left[\left.\log M_{s}\right|_{F^{n}_{\infty}H^{n+q}(X_{\infty})_{W}}\right],

\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}}(g)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{q\geq 0,\,W\in\widehat{G}}(-1)^{q}\frac{\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}{\rm Tr}\left[\left.\log M_{s}\right|_{F^{n}_{\infty}H^{n+q}(X_{\infty})_{W}}\right].

Proof.

Since
F^{n}_{\infty}H^{n+q}(X_{\infty})\cong\bigoplus_{W\in\widehat{G}}F^{n}_{\infty}H^{n+q}(X_{\infty})_{W}

as
G
-modules, the result follows from
[
18
, Prop. 2.10]
.
∎

Corollary 5.8
.

If
(\xi,h_{\xi})|_{X}
is the trivial line bundle endowed with the trivial metric, the coefficient of the logarithmic singularity of

\log\tau_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)
is given by

\kappa_{g}=\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}}(g)+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{q\geq 0,\,W\in\widehat{G}}(-1)^{q}\frac{\chi_{W}(g)}{\dim W}{\rm Tr}\left[\left.\log M_{s}\right|_{F^{n}_{\infty}H^{n+q}(X_{\infty})_{W}}\right].

Proof.

The result follows from (
5.1
) and Proposition
5.7

∎

5.2.2.
The case of isolated hypersurface singularities

In Subsection
5.2.2
, we assume that
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
consists of isolated points.
We recall two invariants of isolated hypersurface singularities.
In what follows, we identify an isolated hypersurface singularity germ
(X_{0},0)\subset({\mathbf{C}}^{n+1},0)

with its defining equation
f(z)=0
, where
f(z)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{n+1},0}
has an isolated critical point at the origin.
We denote by
\mu(f)
the Milnor number of
f
.

Very recently, another invariant of
f
called
spectral genus
is introduced by Eriksson-Freixas i Montplet
[
19
]
.
Let
{\rm Mil}_{f}
be the Milnor fiber of
f
. By Steenbrink
[
41
, Sect. 3]
,

H^{n}({\rm Mil}_{f})
carries a mixed Hodge structure. Let
F^{\bullet}H^{n}({\rm Mil}_{f})
be the Hodge filtration on
H^{n}({\rm Mil}_{f})
.
As before, let
M_{s}\in{\rm GL}(H^{n}({\rm Mil}_{f}))
be the semi-simple part of the monodromy acting on
H^{n}({\rm Mil}_{f})
.
Recall that
\log z
is the branch of the logarithm, whose imaginary part lies in
[0,2\pi)
.
Let
\log M_{s}
be the corresponding logarithm of
M_{s}
. Since
M_{s}
preserves
F^{\bullet}H^{n}({\rm Mil}_{f})
,

\log M_{s}
acts on
{\rm Gr}_{F}^{n}H^{n}({\rm Mil}_{f})
. By
[
19
]
,
the spectral genus of
f
is defined as the rational number

\widetilde{p}_{g}(f):=(2\pi i)^{-1}{\rm Tr}\left[\left.\log M_{s}\right|_{{\rm Gr}_{F}^{n}H^{n}({\rm Mil}_{f})}\right].

We refer the reader to
[
19
]
for further details about the spectral genus.

The following formula for the asymptotic of the analytic torsion was obtained by Eriksson-Freixas i Montplet
[
19
, Cor. 3.2]
.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
\pi_{x}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
is the germ defined by
\pi
at
x\in X
. As
s\to 0
,

\log\tau(X_{s},K_{X_{s}})=-\{\sum_{x\in{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}}\frac{\mu(\pi_{x})}{(n+2)!}-\widetilde{p}_{g}(\pi_{x})\}\log|s|^{2}+O\left(\log\log(|s|^{-2})\right).

Proof.

The result follows from
[
19
, Cor. 3.2]
and (
5.8
).
∎

5.3.
Canonical singularities and
L^{2}
-metrics

In Section
5.3
, we prove the continuity of the
L^{2}
-metric on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
when
X_{0}
has canonical singularities.
For this sake, we recall some notions concerning singularities and introduce some notations used throughout Section
5.3
.
Let
V
be a normal variety with canonical divisor
K_{V}
and let
\phi\colon\widetilde{V}\to V
be any resolution.
Then
V
has canonical singularities if
K_{V}
is
{\mathbf{Q}}
-Cartier and
K_{\widetilde{V}}-\phi^{*}K_{V}
is effective.

Consider the pair
(X,X_{0})
. Recall that
\beta\colon X^{\prime}\to X
is an embedded log resolution of
X_{0}
.
Let
E=\sum_{i\in I}a_{i}D_{i}\subset X^{\prime}
be a
\beta
-exceptional normal crossing divisor with

(5.9)

K_{X^{\prime}}+\widetilde{X}_{0}=\beta^{*}(K_{X}+X_{0})+E.

Here
\widetilde{X}_{0}\subset X^{\prime}
is the proper transform of
X_{0}
, which is a smooth hypersurfaces of
X^{\prime}
,

D_{i}
is a prime divisor of
X^{\prime}
,
a_{i}\in{\mathbf{Z}}
(
i\in I
),
and
X^{\prime}_{0}:=\beta^{-1}(X_{0})
is a normal crossing divisor of
X^{\prime}
with
\widetilde{X}_{0}\cup E\subset X^{\prime}_{0}
.
The pair
(X,X_{0})
is called canonical (resp. log canonical) if
a_{i}\geq 0
(resp.
a_{i}\geq-1
) for all
i\in I
(
[
31
, Def. 3.5]
).
If
X_{0}
is reduced and has only canonical singularities, then
(X,X_{0})
is canonical (
[
42
]
,
[
31
, Th. 7.9]
).

5.3.1.
Fiber integrals and canonical singularities

Let
\omega\in A^{n+1,n+1}(X)
. We can express
\omega=\pi^{*}(ds\wedge d\bar{s})\wedge R
, where

R\in C^{\infty}(X\setminus{\rm Sing}\,X_{0},\Omega_{X/S}^{n}\wedge\overline{\Omega_{X/S}^{n}})
.
Define
{\mathcal{R}}(s)\in C^{\infty}(S^{o})
as the fiber integral
\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}(s):=\int_{X_{s}}R|_{X_{s}}
.
Then
\pi_{*}(\omega)={\mathcal{R}}(s)\,ds\wedge d\bar{s}
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Suppose that
X_{0}
is a reduced divisor with only canonical singularities. Then
{\mathcal{R}}(s)
lies in
{\mathcal{B}}(S)

by setting
\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}(0):=\int_{(X_{0})_{\rm reg}}R|_{(X_{0})_{\rm reg}}
.

Proof.

(Step 1)

Set
\pi^{\prime}:=\pi\circ\beta
. Then
X^{\prime}_{0}=(\pi^{\prime})^{-1}(0)
and
X^{\prime}_{s}:=(\pi^{\prime})^{-1}(s)\cong X_{s}
for
s\in S^{o}
.
Let
{\mathcal{V}}\subset X
be a relatively compact open subset carrying a nowhere vanishing holomorphic
n+1
-form
\eta
.
By an argument using partition of unity, it suffices to prove the assertion when
\omega
has support in
{\mathcal{V}}
.
So we suppose
{\rm supp}\,\omega\subset{\mathcal{V}}
. Let
p\in X^{\prime}_{0}\cap\beta^{-1}({\mathcal{V}})
.
Since
X_{0}
is a reduced divisor of
X
, there is a coordinate neighborhood
(U,(z_{0},\cdots,z_{n}))
centered at
p
satisfying

\beta(U)\subset{\mathcal{V}}
and one of the following (a), (b):
Write
\pi^{\prime}|_{U}(z)=z_{0}^{e_{0}}\cdots z_{n}^{e_{n}}
,
e_{i}\in{\bf Z}_{\geq 0}
.

(a)

If
p\in\widetilde{X}_{0}
, then
X^{\prime}_{s}\cap U=\{z\in U;\,z_{0}z_{1}^{e_{1}}\cdots z_{n}^{e_{n}}=s\}
and
\widetilde{X}_{0}\cap U=\{z_{0}=0\}
.

(b)

If
p\not\in\widetilde{X}_{0}
, then

X^{\prime}_{s}\cap U=\{z\in U;\,z_{0}^{e_{0}}z_{1}^{e_{1}}\cdots z_{n}^{e_{n}}=s\}
,
e_{0}>0
and
\widetilde{X}_{0}\cap U=\emptyset
.

Then, on
U
, we can express

(5.10)

(\pi^{\prime})^{*}\left(\frac{ds}{s}\right)=\beta^{*}\pi^{*}\left(\frac{ds}{s}\right)=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{lll}\frac{dz_{0}}{z_{0}}+\sum_{i\geq 1}e_{i}\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}&{\rm if}&p\in\widetilde{X}_{0},\\
\sum_{i\geq 0}e_{i}\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}&{\rm if}&p\not\in\widetilde{X}_{0}.\end{array}\end{cases}

(Step 2)

Since
X_{0}
has only canonical singularities, the pair
(X,X_{0})
is canonical.
Namely,
a_{i}\geq 0
for all
i\in I
in (
5.9
).
Since
\eta/\pi^{*}s
is a meromorphic
(n+1)
-form with pole of order
1
along
X_{0}\cap{\mathcal{V}}
,

\beta^{*}(\eta/\pi^{*}s)
is a meromorphic
(n+1)
-form on
X^{\prime}\cap\beta^{-1}({\mathcal{V}})
with at most logarithmic pole along

\widetilde{X}_{0}\cap\beta^{-1}({\mathcal{V}})
,
because
K_{X^{\prime}}+\widetilde{X}_{0}=\beta^{*}(K_{X}+X_{0})+\sum_{i\in I}a_{i}D_{i}
with
a_{i}\geq 0
.
Hence, on
U
, we have

(5.11)

\beta^{*}\left(\frac{\eta}{\pi^{*}s}\right)=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{lll}a(z)\,\frac{dz_{0}}{z_{0}}\wedge dz_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}&\text{if}&p\in\widetilde{X}_{0}\cap U,\\
b(z)\,dz_{0}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}&\text{if}&p\not\in\widetilde{X}_{0}\cap U,\end{array}\end{cases}

where
a(z),b(z)\in{\mathcal{O}}(U)
. Let
\widetilde{\eta}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{V}}\setminus X_{0},\Omega^{n}_{X/S})
be such that

\eta=\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\pi^{*}ds
. Since

(5.12)

\beta^{*}(\eta/\pi^{*}s)=\beta^{*}\widetilde{\eta}\wedge(\pi^{\prime})^{*}(ds/s),

\beta^{*}\widetilde{\eta}
is expressed as follows on
U\setminus X^{\prime}_{0}
by
(
5.10
), (
5.11
), (
5.12
):

(5.13)

\beta^{*}\widetilde{\eta}=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{lll}a(z)\,dz_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\mod(\pi^{\prime})^{*}ds&\text{if}&p\in\widetilde{X}_{0}\cap U,\\
\frac{z_{0}}{e_{0}}b(z)\,dz_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}\mod(\pi^{\prime})^{*}ds&\text{if}&p\not\in\widetilde{X}_{0}\cap U.\end{array}\end{cases}

In (
5.11
), (
5.13
), set
c(z):=a(z)
in case (a) and
c(z):=z_{0}b(z)/e_{0}
in case (b).
Then
c(z)\in{\mathcal{O}}(U)
.
Set
\tau:=dz_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}
. By (
5.13
), for all
\chi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(U)
and
s\in S^{o}
,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{X^{\prime}_{s}\cap U}\chi\,\beta^{*}(\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\eta}})|_{X^{\prime}_{s}\cap U}=\int_{X^{\prime}_{s}\cap U}\chi\,|c|^{2}\,\tau\wedge\overline{\tau}
. It follows from
[
2
, Th. 1]
that

(5.14)

\lim_{s\to 0}\int_{X^{\prime}_{s}\cap U}\chi\,\beta^{*}(\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\eta}})=\int_{X^{\prime}_{0}\cap U}\chi\,|c|^{2}\,\tau\wedge\overline{\tau}=\int_{\widetilde{X}_{0}\cap U}\chi\,|c|^{2}\,\tau\wedge\overline{\tau}=\int_{\widetilde{X}_{0}\cap U}\chi\,\beta^{*}(\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\eta}}).

Here we get the second equality as follows.
In case (a),
(X^{\prime}_{0}\setminus\widetilde{X}_{0})\cap U
is defined locally by the equation
z_{1}^{e_{1}}\cdots z_{n}^{e_{n}}=0
.
Hence one of
dz_{1},\ldots,dz_{n}
vanishes on
(X^{\prime}_{0}\setminus\widetilde{X}_{0})\cap U
,
which implies the second equality of (
5.14
).
In case (b), let
x\in(X^{\prime}_{0}\setminus\widetilde{X}_{0})\cap U
.
Then one of
z_{0},\ldots,z_{n}
vanishes on a neighborhood
W
of
x
in
(X^{\prime}_{0}\setminus\widetilde{X}_{0})\cap U
.
If
z_{0}|_{W}=0
, then
c|_{W}=(z_{0}b/e_{0})|_{W}=0
. If
z_{j}|_{W}=0
for some
j>0
, then
dz_{j}|_{W}=0
.
Since
(X^{\prime}_{0}\setminus\widetilde{X}_{0})\cap U
is covered by these
W
,
we get
\int_{(X^{\prime}_{0}\setminus\widetilde{X}_{0})\cap U}F\,|c|^{2}\,\tau\wedge\overline{\tau}=0
.

(Step 3)

Since
\omega\in A^{n+1,n+1}(X)
has compact support in
{\mathcal{V}}
,
there exists a smooth function
m\in C_{0}^{\infty}({\mathcal{V}})
such that

\omega=(-1)^{n}m\,\eta\wedge\overline{\eta}=\pi^{*}(ds\wedge d\bar{s})\wedge m\,\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\eta}}
.
Then
{\mathcal{R}}(s)=\pi_{*}(m\,\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\eta}})
.
Since
\pi_{*}\omega=(\pi\circ\beta)_{*}(\beta^{*}m\cdot\beta^{*}(\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\eta}}))\,ds\wedge d\bar{s}
,
it follows from
[
2
, Th. 1]
that
{\mathcal{R}}(s)
lies in
{\mathcal{B}}(S)

by setting
{\mathcal{R}}(0)=\lim_{s\to 0}{\mathcal{R}}(s)
,
because the integrand of this fiber integral is a smooth
(n,n)
-form on
\beta^{-1}({\mathcal{V}})
by (
5.13
).
By (
5.14
), we get

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to 0}{\mathcal{R}}(s)=\int_{(X_{0})_{\rm reg}}m\,\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\eta}}|_{(X_{0})_{\rm reg}}=\int_{(X_{0})_{\rm reg}}R|_{(X_{0})_{\rm reg}}
.
∎

Remark 5.11
.

For
q=0
, the assertion of Lemma
5.10
holds true even when
\dim T>1
by Takayama
[
43
, Prop. 3.2.3 (2)]
.

5.3.2.
Canonical singularities and the continuity of the
L^{2}
-metric

Theorem 5.12
.

Suppose that
X_{0}
is reduced and has only canonical singularities.
Then, for all
q\geq 0
, the elementary exponents of
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
vanish,
and the
L^{2}
metric on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)|_{S^{o}}
extends to a continuous Hermitian metric on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)|_{S}

of class
{\mathcal{B}}(S)
.

Proof.

After shrinking
S
if necessary, we have sections
\Psi_{1},\ldots,\Psi_{h^{q}}\in H^{q}(X,K_{X}(\xi))
such that

R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X}(\xi)|_{S}={\mathcal{O}}_{S}\Psi_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\Psi_{h^{q}}
.
Set

(5.15)

g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(s):=(\Psi_{\alpha}\otimes(\pi^{*}ds)^{-1}|_{X_{s}},\Psi_{\beta}\otimes(\pi^{*}ds)^{-1}|_{X_{s}})_{L^{2}},\qquad s\in S^{o}.

Then
g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\in C^{\infty}(S^{o})
and
\det(g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}})>0
on
S^{o}
.
It suffices to prove that
g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\in{\mathcal{B}}(S)
and
\det(g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}})>0
on
S
.
By Theorem
2.1
(1), (2), there exist
\psi_{\alpha}\in\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X}^{n-q+1}(\xi))
and

\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha}\in\Gamma(X\setminus{\rm Sing}\,X_{0},\Omega_{X/S}^{n-q}(\xi))
such that

\Psi_{\alpha}=[\psi_{\alpha}\wedge\kappa_{X}^{q}]
and

\psi_{\alpha}|_{X\setminus{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}}=\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha}\wedge\pi^{*}ds
.
Under the canonical identification
K_{X/S}(\xi)|_{X\setminus X_{0}}=\Omega_{X/S}^{n}(\xi)|_{X\setminus X_{0}}
,
in
H^{q}(X\setminus X_{0},K_{X/S}(\xi))
, we have

(5.16)

\Psi_{\alpha}\otimes(\pi^{*}ds)^{-1}=[\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha}\wedge\kappa_{X}^{q}],\qquad\alpha=1,\ldots,h^{q}.

Since
(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha}\wedge\kappa_{X}^{q})|_{X_{s}}
is the harmonic representative of the cohomology class

\Psi_{\alpha}\otimes(\pi^{*}ds)^{-1}|_{X_{s}}

(s\in S^{o})
by Theorem
2.1
(3),
we deduce from (
5.15
), (
5.16
) that

(5.17)

\pi_{*}(i^{(n-q+1)^{2}}h_{\xi}(\psi_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\psi}_{\beta})\wedge\kappa_{X}^{q})=i\,g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(s)\,ds\wedge d\bar{s}.

Since
\psi_{\alpha}\in\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X}^{n-q+1}(\xi))
, we see that

h_{\xi}(\psi_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\psi_{\beta}})\wedge\kappa_{X}^{q}
is a smooth top form on
X
.
By Lemma
5.10
and (
5.17
), we get
g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(s)\in{\mathcal{B}}(S)
.

Consider the semi-stable reduction (
4.1
). By (
4.29
) and Proposition
4.9
,
there exist
\delta^{q}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
C>0
such that

(5.18)

\det(g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}})(\mu(t))=|t|^{-2\delta^{q}}\det\widetilde{H}(t)\geq C\,|t|^{-2\delta^{q}}.

Since
\delta^{q}\geq 0
, there exists
\epsilon>0
such that for all
s\in S^{o}
,
\det(g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(s))\geq\epsilon>0
.
Since
g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(s)\in{\mathcal{B}}(S)
, this implies
\det(g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(0))\geq\epsilon>0
.
Since the left hand side of (
5.18
) extends to a continuous function on
T
, we get
\delta^{q}=0
.
By (
4.28
), this implies the vanishing of the elementary exponents of
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

Let
\sigma_{W}
be a nowhere vanishing holomorphic section of
\lambda_{W}(K_{X/S}(\xi))
(cf. Sect.
1.3.2
).
Then, under the same assumption as in Theorem
5.12
,
\delta_{W}^{q}=\varrho_{W}^{q}=0

for all
q\geq 0
and
W\in\widehat{G}
. In particular,
there exist
c\in{\mathbf{R}}
,
r\in{\mathbf{Q}}_{>0}
and
\nu\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
such that as
s\to 0

\log\|\sigma_{W}^{q}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=c+O\left(|s|^{r}(\log|s|)^{\nu}\right).

Proof.

Since
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)_{W}\subset R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
is a holomorphic subbundle,
the result follows from Theorem
5.12
.
∎

Theorem 5.14
.

Suppose that
X_{0}
is reduced and has only canonical singularities.
Then there exist
\gamma_{g}\in{\mathbf{C}}
,
r\in{\mathbf{Q}}_{>0}
and
\nu\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
such that as
s\to 0
,

\log\tau_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)=\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)\log|s|^{2}+\gamma_{g}+O\left(|s|^{r}(\log(|s|^{-1}))^{\nu}\right).

Proof.

Since
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)=\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=0
by (
4.32
), (
5.2
) and Corollary
5.13
,
the result follows from Theorem
5.1
.
∎

For an example where
\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)
is explicitly evaluated, we refer the reader to
[
50
, Th. 6.3]
.
In
[
27
,
54
]
, we will use Theorem
5.14
to determine certain
holomorphic torsion invariants of Calabi-Yau manifolds with group action.

5.3.3.
Log-canonical singularities and
L^{2}
-metrics

We keep the notation in the proof of Theorem
5.12
.

Proposition 5.15
.

Suppose that
X_{0}
is reduced and the pair
(X,X_{0})
is log-canonical.
Then the elementary exponents of
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
vanish.
Moreover, there exist constants
\varrho^{q}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
c^{q}\in{\mathbf{R}}
such that as
s\to 0
,

(5.19)

\log\det(g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(s))=\varrho^{q}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c^{q}+O(1/\log|s|^{-1}).

Proof.

Recall that
(U,(z_{0},\ldots,z_{n}))
is a coordinate neighborhood as in (Step 1) in the proof of Lemma
5.10
,
that
\eta
is a nowhere vanishing holomorphic
n+1
-form on
U
, and that
\widetilde{\eta}
is a relative
n
-form such that

\eta=\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\pi^{*}ds
.
Since the pair
(X,X_{0})
is log-canonical, by (
5.11
),
\beta^{*}(\eta/\pi^{*}s)
is expressed as follows
on
U\setminus X^{\prime}_{0}
:

(5.20)

\beta^{*}\left(\frac{\eta}{\pi^{*}s}\right)=c(z)\frac{dz_{0}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}}{z_{0}\cdots z_{n}}=\pi^{\prime*}(ds)\wedge c(z)\frac{dz_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_{n}}{z_{1}\cdots z_{n}},

where
c(z)\in{\mathcal{O}}(U)
. By (
5.12
), (
5.20
), for any smooth function
\chi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(U)

with compact support in
U
, there is a constant
C>0
such that

(5.21)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{X^{\prime}_{s}\cap U}\chi\,\beta^{*}(\widetilde{\eta}\wedge\overline{\widetilde{\eta}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\,\left|\int_{z\in U,\,z_{0}^{e_{1}}\cdots z_{n}^{e_{n}}=s}\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{n}}{z_{n}}\wedge\overline{\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_{n}}{z_{n}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\,(\log|s|^{-1})^{n}.

By (
5.17
) and the smoothness of the top form

h_{\xi}(\psi_{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\psi}_{\beta})\wedge\kappa_{X}^{q}
,
we deduce from (
5.21
) that
g_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}(s)=O\left((\log|s|^{-1})^{n}\right)
.
This implies that
D(t)
in Theorem
4.12
(1) is the identity matrix. Namely,
e_{1}^{q}=\cdots=e_{h_{W}^{q}}^{q}=0
.
Since
W
is arbitrary, this implies the vanishing of the elementary exponents of
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
.
By (
4.28
), we get
\delta_{W}^{q}=0
in (
4.26
).
Then (
5.19
) follows from (
4.26
). This completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 5.16
.

Suppose that
X_{0}
is reduced and the pair
(X,X_{0})
has only log-canonical singularities.
Then there exist
\gamma_{g}\in{\mathbf{C}}
such that as
s\to 0
,

\log\tau_{G}(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))(g)=\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}(g)\log|s|^{2}-\varrho_{g}\,\log\log(|s|^{-2})+\gamma_{g}+O\left(1/\log|s|^{-1}\right).

In particular, if
X_{0}
is a reduced normal crossing divisor of
X
, this formula holds.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.15
, the elementary exponents of
R\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
vanish. The result follows from Theorem
5.1
and (
5.1
).
∎

5.3.4.
Example

Consider the case
g=1
.
Suppose that
\pi(z)
is locally given by a quadratic polynomial of rank two near the critical locus
\Sigma=\Sigma_{\pi}
, i.e.,
for any
p\in\Sigma
,

\pi(z)=z_{0}z_{1}

in a suitable local coordinates
z=(z_{0},\ldots,z_{n})
centered at
p
.
(Typical examples of such degenerations are provided by the deformation to the normal cone e.g.
[
8
, Sect. 8 a)]
.)
Then the pair
(X,X_{0})
is log-canonical and Theorem
5.16
is applicable to this family.
To give an explicit formula for
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
in this case, recall that the Bismut
E
-genus

[
8
, Def. 5.1, Prop. 5.2]
is the additive characteristic class associated to the power series

{\rm E}(x):=\frac{x-{\rm sinh}(x)}{2x(1-{\rm cosh}(x))}=\frac{{\rm Td}(x){\rm Td}(-x)}{2x}\left(\frac{{\rm Td}^{-1}(x)-1}{x}-\frac{{\rm Td}^{-1}(-x)-1}{-x}\right).

Then
{\rm E}(0)=1/6
.

Proposition 5.17
.

Under the assumption as above, the following identity holds

\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}{\rm Td}(T\Sigma){\rm E}(N_{\Sigma/X}){\rm ch}(K_{\Sigma}(\xi)).

Proof.

We use the notation in Section
3.4
. Let
q\colon\widetilde{X}\to X
be the blowing-up of
\Sigma
and
let
E=q^{-1}(\Sigma)\cong{\mathbf{P}}(N_{\Sigma/X})
be the exceptional divisor. Then the indeterminacy of the Gauss map for
\pi

can be resolved by considering
\widetilde{X}
.
Let
p\colon{\mathbf{P}}(N_{\Sigma/X})\to\Sigma
be the projection. Since
q|_{E}=p
, setting
g=1
in (
3.8
), we get

\displaystyle\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}

\displaystyle=\int_{E}\widetilde{\gamma}^{*}\left\{\frac{{\rm Td}^{-1}({\mathcal{H}}^{\lor})-1}{c_{1}({\mathcal{H}}^{\lor})}\right\}p^{*}\left\{{\rm Td}(TX)e^{-c_{1}(X)}{\rm ch}(\xi)\right\}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}p_{*}\widetilde{\gamma}^{*}\left\{\frac{{\rm Td}^{-1}({\mathcal{H}}^{\lor})-1}{c_{1}({\mathcal{H}}^{\lor})}\right\}{\rm Td}(N_{\Sigma/X})e^{-c_{1}(N_{\Sigma/X})}{\rm Td}(T\Sigma){\rm ch}(K_{\Sigma}(\xi)).

Let
F:={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}(N_{\Sigma/X})}(1)
.
By
[
51
, (23)]
,
F=\widetilde{\gamma}^{*}{\mathcal{H}}|_{E}
. Set
f(x):=({\rm Td}^{-1}(x)-1)/x
and

f_{-}(x):=(f(x)-f(-x))/2x
. Then we have

p_{*}\widetilde{\gamma}^{*}\left\{\frac{{\rm Td}^{-1}({\mathcal{H}}^{\lor})-1}{c_{1}({\mathcal{H}}^{\lor})}\right\}=p_{*}(f(-c_{1}(F)))=-p_{*}(f_{*}(c_{1}(F)))=-\frac{1}{2}f_{-}(N_{\Sigma/X}),

where the second equality follows from
[
51
, p.80 last line]
and the third equality follows from
[
51
, p.81]
.
Since
c_{1}(N_{\Sigma/X})=0
by
[
8
, (2.9)]
, this implies that

(5.22)

\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}f_{-}(N_{\Sigma/X}){\rm Td}(N_{\Sigma/X}){\rm Td}(T\Sigma){\rm ch}(K_{\Sigma}(\xi)).

Since
f_{-}(N_{\Sigma/X}){\rm Td}(N_{\Sigma/X})={\rm E}(N_{\Sigma/X})
by
[
51
, (29)]
,
the desired formula follows from (
5.22
).
∎

6.
Asymptotic behavior of Bott-Chern forms under degenerations

In Sections
6
and
7
, we provide a topological formula for the coefficient
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}

in Theorem
5.1
. To this end, in this section,
we study the asymptotic behavior of the fiber integrals of Bott-Chern forms under certain degeneracy assumption.
In the remainder of this paper, we assume

G=\{1\}.

6.1.
Bott-Chern forms

We introduce the notation used throughout Section
6
.
Let
M
be a complex manifold.
Let
E
,
E^{\prime}
be holomorphic vector bundles on
M
of rank
r
. Let

\varphi\colon E\to E^{\prime}

be a homomorphism of vector bundles. Let
N
be the degeneracy locus of
\varphi
.
Namely,
N:=\{x\in M;\,{\rm rank}(\varphi_{x})<r\}
. We assume that
N
is a compact hypersurface of
M
.
We set
M^{0}:=M\setminus N
.
Let
h_{E}
and
h_{E^{\prime}}
be Hermitian metrics on
E
and
E^{\prime}
, respectively.
We set
\overline{E}:=(E,h_{E})
and
\overline{E^{\prime}}:=(E^{\prime},h_{E^{\prime}})
.
Let
R_{\overline{E}}
and
R_{\overline{E^{\prime}}}
be the curvature forms of
\overline{E}
and
\overline{E^{\prime}}

with respect to the Chern connection, respectively.
Then
\varphi
induces an isomorphism of holomorphic Hermitian vector bundles on
M^{0}

\overline{E^{\prime}}\cong(E,h_{E^{\prime}}^{\varphi}),\qquad h_{E^{\prime}}^{\varphi}(u,v):=h_{E^{\prime}}(\varphi(u),\varphi(v)).

Throughout this section, we assume that

P(\cdot)\in\mathfrak{gl}({\bf C}^{r})^{{\rm GL}({\bf C}^{r})}
is a
{\rm GL}({\mathbf{C}}^{r})
-invariant polynomial
on the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{gl}({\bf C}^{r})

with respect to the adjoint action. We set

P(\overline{E})=P(E,h_{E}):=P\left(-R_{\overline{E}}/2\pi i\right).

By
[
10
, I, e), f)]
,
[
24
, Sect.1.2.4]
, we have the Bott-Chern class

\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}}):=\widetilde{P}(E;h_{E},\varphi^{*}h_{E^{\prime}})\in\widetilde{A}(M^{0})

such that

dd^{c}\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})=P(\overline{E})-P(\overline{E^{\prime}}).

In this section, we construct a natural extension of
\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})
to a current on
M
.
For later use in the next section, when
M
admits a proper holomorphic function
f\colon M\to\varDelta
with
N\subset f^{-1}(0)
,
we study the asymptotic behavior of the fiber integral

f_{*}\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})
as a function on the punctured disc
\varDelta^{*}
.
We remark that, when
N
is a smooth hypersurface and
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{M}(E)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{M}(E^{\prime})
provides
a resolution of
i_{*}(E^{\prime}|_{N})
with
i\colon N\hookrightarrow M
being an embedding,
a natural extension of
\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})
to a current on
M
was constructed by
Bismut, Gillet, and Soulé
[
12
]
. While their construction extends to general immersions of higher codimension,
it is not applicable to our setting because
N
is not necessarily smooth.

We introduce a particular representative of the Bott-Chern class
\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})
,
which we use in Section
6
. To this end, we recall the construction of the Bott-Chern class given in
[
10
, I, f)]
,

[
24
, Sect. 1.2.3]
. (See
[
10
, I, Cor. 1.30]
for another construction.)
Let
v\colon F\to F^{\prime}
be an isomorphism of holomorphic vector bundles on
M
.
Let
h_{F}
,
h_{F^{\prime}}
be Hermitian metrics on
F
,
F^{\prime}
, respectively.
Let
\pi_{1}\colon M\times{\bf P}^{1}\to M
and
\pi_{2}\colon M\times{\bf P}^{1}\to{\bf P}^{1}
be the natural projections.
Set
\widetilde{F}:=\pi_{1}^{*}F
. Define a Hermitian metric
h_{\widetilde{F}}
on
\widetilde{F}
as

h_{\widetilde{F}}:=\frac{1}{1+|\zeta|^{2}}h_{F}+\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{1+|\zeta|^{2}}h_{F^{\prime}}^{v},

where
\zeta
is the inhomogeneous coordinate of
{\mathbf{P}}^{1}
.
Then
h_{\widetilde{F}}|_{M\times\{0\}}=h_{F}
and
\widetilde{h}_{\widetilde{F}}|_{M\times\{\infty\}}=v^{*}h_{F^{\prime}}=h_{F^{\prime}}^{v}
.
Setting
S=0
,
E=F
,
Q=F^{\prime}
in
[
24
, Sect. 1.2.3]
,
we deduce from
[
24
, (1.2.3.2)]
that in
\widetilde{A}_{M}
,

(6.1)

\widetilde{P}(\overline{F},\overline{F^{\prime}})=\int_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}P\left(\frac{i}{2\pi}R(\widetilde{F},h_{\widetilde{F}})\right)\,\log|\zeta|^{2}.

(We remark that we set
S=0
,
E=F
,
Q=F^{\prime}
here, while
S=F
,
E=F^{\prime}
,
Q=0
in
[
24
, Sect. 1.2.4]
.
This explains the difference of the sign between (
6.1
) and
[
24
, (1.2.3.2)]
.)
Under the identification
(F^{\prime},h_{F^{\prime}})\cong(F,h_{F^{\prime}}^{v})
,

\widetilde{P}(\overline{F},\overline{F^{\prime}})
is also denoted by
\widetilde{P}(F;h_{F},h_{F^{\prime}})
.
In what follows, the representative of
\widetilde{P}(\overline{F},\overline{F^{\prime}})
given by (
6.1
)
is called the Bott-Chern form and is still denoted by the same symbol
\widetilde{P}(\overline{F},\overline{F^{\prime}})
.

6.2.
The Grassmann bundle and the section associated to
\varphi

Let
\sigma_{0},\sigma_{\infty}\in H^{0}({\bf P}^{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\bf P}^{1}}(1))
be the canonical sections such that

{\rm div}(\sigma_{0})=0
,
{\rm div}(\sigma_{\infty})=\infty
. Then
\zeta=\sigma_{0}/\sigma_{\infty}
.
Let
h_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\bf P}^{1}}(1)}
be the standard Hermitian metric on
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\bf P}^{1}}(1)
with

\|\sigma_{0}(\zeta)\|^{2}=|\zeta|^{2}/(1+|\zeta|^{2}),\qquad\|\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta)\|^{2}=1/(1+|\zeta|^{2}).

Set

E(1):=\pi_{1}^{*}E\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\bf P}^{1}}(1),\qquad E^{\prime}(1):=\pi_{1}^{*}E^{\prime}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\bf P}^{1}}(1).

Then
E(1)
and
E^{\prime}(1)
are endowed with the product Hermitian metrics

h_{E(1)}=\pi_{1}^{*}h_{E}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}h_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\bf P}^{1}}(1)}
and

h_{E^{\prime}(1)}=\pi_{1}^{*}h_{E^{\prime}}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}h_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\bf P}^{1}}(1)}
, respectively.

Let
\varepsilon_{\varphi}\colon\pi_{1}^{*}E\to E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1)
be the homomorphism of vector bundles defined as

(6.2)

(\varepsilon_{\varphi})_{(x,\zeta)}(e):=(e\otimes\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta),\varphi_{x}(e)\otimes\sigma_{0}(\zeta)),\qquad(x,\zeta)\in M\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1},\quad e\in E_{x}.

Define a holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle
\overline{\mathcal{E}}
on
(M\times{\bf P}^{1})\setminus(N\times\{\infty\})
as

\overline{\mathcal{E}}:=(\pi_{1}^{*}E,\|\sigma_{\infty}\|^{2}h_{E}+\|\sigma_{0}\|^{2}h_{E^{\prime}}^{\varphi}).

Then we have an isomorphism of holomorphic Hermitian vector bundles

\overline{\mathcal{E}}\cong(\varepsilon_{\varphi}(\pi_{1}^{*}E),(h_{E(1)}\oplus h_{E^{\prime}(1)})|_{\varepsilon_{\varphi}(E)})

on
(M\times{\bf P}^{1})\setminus(N\times\{\infty\})
. In particular, for
\zeta\not=\infty
, we have

(6.3)

\left(E,\frac{1}{1+|\zeta|^{2}}h_{E}+\frac{|\zeta|^{2}}{1+|\zeta|^{2}}h_{E^{\prime}}^{\varphi}\right)\cong(\varepsilon_{\varphi}(\pi_{1}^{*}E),(h_{E(1)}\oplus h_{E^{\prime}(1)})|_{\varepsilon_{\varphi}(E)})|_{M\times\{\zeta\}}

and for
\zeta=\infty
, we have

(E|_{M^{0}},h_{E^{\prime},\varphi})\cong(\varepsilon_{\varphi}(\pi_{1}^{*}E),(h_{E(1)}\oplus h_{E^{\prime}(1)})|_{\varepsilon_{\varphi}(E)})|_{M^{0}\times\{\infty\}}=\overline{E^{\prime}}|_{M^{0}}.

Let
\varPi\colon{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))\to M\times{\bf P}^{1}
be the Grassmann bundle such that

{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))_{(x,\zeta)}=\varPi^{-1}(x,\zeta):={\rm Gr}(r,E(1)_{(x,\zeta)}\oplus E^{\prime}(1)_{(x,\zeta)})

for
(x,\zeta)\in M\times{\bf P}^{1}
. Hence
{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))
parametrizes linear subspacess of dimension
r

of the fibers of
E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1)
.

Let

{\mathscr{U}}:={\mathscr{U}}^{E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1)}\to{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))

be the universal vector bundle of rank
r
.
Since
{\mathscr{U}}\subset\varPi^{*}(E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))
,
{\mathscr{U}}
is endowed with the Hermitian metric

h_{\mathscr{U}}:=\varPi^{*}(h_{E(1)}\oplus h_{E^{\prime}(1)})|_{\mathscr{U}}
. We set

\overline{\mathscr{U}}:=({\mathscr{U}},h_{\mathscr{U}}).

Let
\sigma_{\varphi}\colon(M\times{\bf P}^{1})\setminus(N\times\{\infty\})\to{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))
be the holomorphic section
associated to the homomorphism
\varepsilon_{\varphi}
defined by

(6.4)

\sigma_{\varphi}(x,\zeta):=[(\varepsilon_{\varphi})_{(x,\zeta)}(E_{x})].

Here, for a subspace
V\subset E_{x}\oplus E^{\prime}_{x}
of dimension
r
,
[V]\in{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))_{(x,\zeta)}
denotes the point
corresponding to
V
. Since

({\mathscr{U}}_{\sigma_{\varphi}(x,\zeta)},h_{{\mathcal{U}},\sigma_{\varphi}(x,\zeta)})=(\varepsilon_{\varphi}(E_{x}\times\{\zeta\}),h_{E(1)}\oplus h_{E^{\prime}(1)}|_{\varepsilon_{\varphi}(E_{x}\times\{\zeta\})}),

comparing this with (
6.3
), we have

\overline{\mathcal{E}}=\sigma_{\varphi}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{U}}

as holomorphic Hermitian vector bundles on
(M\times{\bf P}^{1})\setminus(N\times\{\infty\})
.
Since
N\times\{\infty\}
has codimension
2
in
M\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1}
,
the holomorphic section
\sigma_{\varphi}
from
(M\times{\bf P}^{1})\setminus(N\times\{\infty\})
to
{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))

extends to a meromorphic map (
[
39
]
).

Fix a resolution of the indeterminacy

q\colon{\mathfrak{M}}\to M\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1}

of the meromorphic map
\sigma_{\varphi}\colon M\times{\bf P}^{1}\dashrightarrow{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))
. Set

(6.5)

{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}:=q^{-1}(N\times\{\infty\}),

which is endowed with the structure of a divisor on
{\mathfrak{M}}
such that

(6.6)

{\rm div}(q^{*}\zeta)^{-1}=\widetilde{M\times\{\infty\}}+{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}-q^{-1}(M\times\{0\}).

Here
\widetilde{M\times\{\infty\}}
is the proper transform of
M\times\{\infty\}
.
Since
N
is compact and
q
is proper,
{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}
is compact.
By definition, there is a holomorphic map

\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}\colon{\mathfrak{M}}\to{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))

such that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}=\sigma_{\varphi}\circ q
on
{\mathfrak{M}}\setminus{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}
.
We have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{{\mathfrak{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}}
\textstyle{N\times\{\infty\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\times{\bf P}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{\varphi}}
\textstyle{{\rm Gr}(r,E(1)\oplus E^{\prime}(1))}

Then
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{U}}
is a holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle on
{\mathfrak{M}}
such that
on
{\mathfrak{M}}\setminus{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}
,

(6.7)

\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{U}}=q^{*}\overline{\mathcal{E}}

By (
6.7
), we get the following equality of differential forms
on
{\mathfrak{M}}\setminus{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}

(6.8)

q^{*}P(\overline{\mathcal{E}})=\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}}),

where
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})\in\bigoplus_{p\geq 0}A^{p,p}({\mathfrak{M}})
.

6.3.
An extension of Bott-Chern forms to currents

Following MacPherson’s graph construction for vector bundle homomorphism
[
23
, Example 18.1.6]

(see also
[
23
, p.341 Eq.(14) and Def. 18.1]
), for a polynomial
P=P(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots)
in the Chern classes, we set

(6.9)

P^{M}_{N}(\varphi):=(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left[c_{1}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi})\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P({\mathscr{U}})\right].

Here
P^{M}_{N}(\varphi)
is regarded as a linear form on
H^{*}_{\rm DR}(M,{\mathbf{C}})
defined as

(6.10)

\displaystyle\int_{M}P^{M}_{N}(\varphi)\wedge\chi

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{M}}c_{1}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi})\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P({\mathscr{U}})\wedge(\pi_{1}\circ q)^{*}\chi

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P({\mathscr{U}})\wedge(\pi_{1}\circ q)^{*}\chi

for
\chi\in H^{*}_{\rm DR}(M,{\mathbf{C}})
. Hence
P^{M}_{N}(\varphi)\in H^{*}_{{\rm DR},c}(M,{\mathbf{C}})
.
By
[
23
, Example 18.1.6 (c)]
, we have the following equality of cohomology classes with compact support:

(6.11)

P(E)-P(E^{\prime})=P^{M}_{N}(\varphi).

In this subsection, using the Bott-Chern forms, we provide a refinement of (
6.11
) at the level of currents.
Recall that on
M^{0}
,
\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})
is given by the smooth form

(6.12)

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{1})_{*}\left[P(\overline{\mathcal{E}})\,\log|\zeta|^{2}\right]=(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left[P(q^{*}\overline{\mathcal{E}})\cdot q^{*}(\log|\zeta|^{2})\right]

\displaystyle=(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left[\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})\cdot q^{*}(\log|\zeta|^{2})\right],

where we used (
6.8
) to get the last expression.
Since
