\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})\cdot q^{*}(\log|\zeta|^{2})
is a differential form on
{\mathfrak{M}}

with coefficients in
L^{1}_{\rm loc}
,
(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}[\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})\cdot q^{*}(\log|\zeta|^{2})]

defines a current on
M
by the properness of
\pi_{1}\circ q
.

Definition 6.1
.

The Bott-Chern type current
\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle
on
M
is defined as

\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle:=(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}[\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})\cdot q^{*}(\log|\zeta|^{2})].

Then (
6.11
) is refined as follows.

Theorem 6.2
.

The following identity of currents on
M
holds:

dd^{c}\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle=P(\overline{E})-P(\overline{E^{\prime}})-(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left[\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})\right].

Here
\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}
is the Dirac
\delta
-current associated to the divisor
{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}
.

Proof.

We remark that since
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})
is a smooth form on
{\mathfrak{M}}
, the product of currents

\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})
is well-defined.
Since
\partial
and
\bar{\partial}
commute with
(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}
and
dd^{c}\log|\zeta|^{2}=\delta_{0}-\delta_{\infty}
, we get

\displaystyle dd^{c}\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle

\displaystyle=(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left[\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})\wedge\{dd^{c}q^{*}(\log|\zeta|^{2})\}\right]

\displaystyle=(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left[\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})\wedge\{\delta_{q^{-1}(M\times\{0\})}-\delta_{\widetilde{M\times\{\infty\}}}-\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\}\right]

\displaystyle=P(\overline{E})-P(\overline{E^{\prime}})-(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left[\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathscr{U}})\wedge\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\right],

where the second equality follows from (
6.6
). This completes the proof.
∎

In certain special cases, an explicit formula for the current
\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle
,
along with a comparison to the Bott-Chern singular currents of Bismut-Gillet-Soulé
[
11
]
,
[
12
]
,
will be presented in Section
9.3
.

6.4.
The asymptotic behavior of the fiber integral
f_{*}[\chi\wedge\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle]

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
\varDelta\subset{\mathbf{C}}
be the unit disc. Let
f\colon M\to\varDelta
be a surjective proper holomorphic function with

N\subset f^{-1}(0)
. Let
\chi
be a
\partial
and
\bar{\partial}
closed smooth differential form on
M
.
Then

f_{*}[\chi\wedge\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle]^{(n,n)}\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}-\left\{\int_{M}P^{M}_{N}(\varphi)\wedge\chi\right\}\,\log|t|^{2}.

Proof.

Set
A:=\int_{M}P^{M}_{N}(\varphi)\wedge\chi
.
By
[
51
, Lemma 4.4]
and Definition
6.1
,
there exist
\alpha\in{\mathbf{R}}
and
g\in{\mathcal{B}}(\varDelta)
such that

f_{*}[\chi\wedge\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle]^{(n,n)}=\alpha\,\log|t|^{2}+g
.
It suffices to prove
\alpha=-A
.
Since
\partial\chi=\bar{\partial}\chi=0
, it follows from Theorem
6.2
and the commutativity of
f_{*}
and
dd^{c}

that as currents on
\varDelta
,

\displaystyle dd^{c}f_{*}[\chi\wedge\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle]^{(n,n)}=f_{*}[\chi\wedge\{dd^{c}\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle\}]^{(n+1,n+1)}

\displaystyle=f_{*}[\chi\wedge\{P(\overline{E})-P(\overline{E^{\prime}})\}]^{(n+1,n+1)}-f_{*}\left[\chi\wedge(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left\{\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathcal{U}})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\equiv-f_{*}\left[\chi\wedge(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left\{\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathcal{U}})\right\}\right]\mod\bigcup_{p>1}L^{p}_{\rm loc}(\varDelta)\cdot dt\wedge d\bar{t}.

Here we used
f_{*}(A^{n+1,n+1}(M))\subset\bigcup_{p>1}L_{\rm loc}^{p}(\varDelta)\cdot dt\wedge d\bar{t}

(
[
51
, Lemma 9.2]
) to get the last equality.
Since
(\pi_{1}\circ q)({\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi})\subset N
and hence
(f\circ\pi_{1}\circ q)({\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi})=\{0\}
by assumption,
we get for all
\varrho\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\varDelta)

\displaystyle\int_{\varDelta}\varrho\cdot f_{*}\left[\chi\wedge(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left\{\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathcal{U}})\right\}\right]=\int_{M}f^{*}\varrho\cdot\chi\wedge(\pi_{1}\circ q)_{*}\left\{\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathcal{U}})\right\}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\{(\pi_{1}\circ q)^{*}(f^{*}\varrho\cdot\chi)\}\wedge\left\{\delta_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathcal{U}})\right\}=\int_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}\{(\pi_{1}\circ q)^{*}(f^{*}\varrho\cdot\chi)\}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathcal{U}})

\displaystyle=\varrho(0)\int_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\varphi}}(\pi_{1}\circ q)^{*}\chi\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varphi}^{*}P(\overline{\mathcal{U}})=A\,\varrho(0)=A\int_{\varDelta}\varrho\,dd^{c}\log|s|^{2}.

Since
dd^{c}{\mathcal{B}}(\varDelta)\subset\bigcup_{p>1}L^{p}_{\rm loc}(\varDelta)\cdot dt\wedge d\bar{t}
and hence

dd^{c}(\alpha\log|t|^{2}+g)\equiv\alpha dd^{c}\log|t|^{2}\mod\bigcup_{p>1}L^{p}_{\rm loc}(\varDelta)\cdot dt\wedge d\bar{t}
,
we get
\alpha=-A
.
∎

6.5.
Bott-Chern type currents for generically injective homomorphisms

For later use in the next section, we determine the asymptotic behavior of the fiber integral of Bott-Chern forms in a more degenerate setting.
Let
(V,h_{V})
be a holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle on
M
of rank
l>r
.
Let
\varPi\colon{\rm Gr}(r,V)\to M
be the Grassmann bundle on
M
such that
{\rm Gr}(r,V)_{x}={\rm Gr}(r,V_{x})
for
x\in M
.
Let

\psi\colon E\to V

be a homomorphism of holomorphic vector bundles on
M
such that
\psi
is injective on
M^{0}
. In particular, it can be possible that

{\rm rk}(\psi_{x})<{\rm rk}(E)
for some
x\in N
. Then

W:=\psi(E)

is a holomorphic subbundle of
V
defined on
M^{0}
endowed with the Hermitian metric
h_{W}:=h_{V}|_{W}
.
We set
\overline{W}:=(W,h_{W})
. On
M^{0}
, we have the Bott-Chern form
\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{W})
.
As before, we construct an extension of
\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{W})
to a current on
M
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Define holomorphic map
{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}\colon M^{0}\to{\rm Gr}(r,V)
by

{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}(x):=[\psi(E_{x})]\in{\rm Gr}(r,V_{x})\qquad(x\in M^{0}).

Then
{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}
extends to a meromorphic map from
M
to
{\rm Gr}(r,V)
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the assertion when
M=\varDelta^{m}
,
E={\mathbf{C}}^{r}
,
V={\mathbf{C}}^{l}
,
and
{\rm Gr}(r,V)={\rm Gr}(r,{\mathbf{C}}^{l})
, where
m=\dim M
.
Fix bases
\{{\mathbf{e}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{e}}_{r}\}
of
{\mathbf{C}}^{r}
and
\{{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\}
of
V
.
Then, for
I=\{i_{1}<\cdots<i_{r}\}
, there exists
\psi_{I}\in{\mathcal{O}}(M)
such that

\Lambda^{r}\psi(x)({\mathbf{e}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge{\mathbf{e}}_{r})=\sum_{|I|=r}\psi_{I}(x)\,{\bf v}_{I}
,
where
\Lambda^{r}\psi(x)\in{\rm Hom}(\Lambda^{r}E_{x},\Lambda^{r}V_{x})
is the homomorphism induced from
\psi
and

{\mathbf{v}}_{I}:={\mathbf{v}}_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge{\mathbf{v}}_{i_{k}}
. Set
N:=\binom{l}{r}
.
By the injectivity of
\psi
on
M^{0}
, there exists an
I
such that
\psi_{I}(x)\not=0
.
By identifying
{\rm Gr}(r,{\bf C}^{l})
with its image in
{\mathbf{P}}^{N-1}
via the Plücker embedding,

{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}
is expressed as the holomorphic map

{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}\colon M^{0}\ni x\to[\cdots:\psi_{I}(x):\cdots]\in{\mathbf{P}}^{N-1}
.
Since
{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}
lifts to the non-constant holomorphic map
M\ni x\to(\ldots,\psi_{I}(x),\ldots)\in{\mathbf{C}}^{N}

from
M
to
{\mathbf{C}}^{N}
,
{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}
extends to a meromorphic map from
M
to
{\mathbf{P}}^{N-1}
.
Since the image of
{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}
is contained in
{\rm Gr}(r,{\mathbf{C}}^{l})
,

{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}
is a meromorphic map from
M
to
{\rm Gr}(r,{\mathbf{C}}^{l})
.
∎

Let

\nu\colon M^{\natural}\to M

be a resolution of the indeterminacy of
{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}
. By definition,

{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural}:={\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}\circ\nu\colon M^{\natural}\setminus\nu^{-1}(N)\to{\rm Gr}(r,V)

extends to a holomorphic map from
M^{\natural}
to
{\rm Gr}(r,V)
.
Let
{\mathcal{U}}^{V}\subset\varPi^{*}V
be the universal rank
r
bundle on
{\rm Gr}(r,V)
.
Since
{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{V}=\psi(E)
on
M^{0}
,

({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{V}
is a holomorphic vector bundle on
M^{\natural}
such that

({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{V}|_{\nu^{-1}(M^{0})}=\nu^{*}(\psi(E))|_{\nu^{-1}(M^{0})}=\nu^{*}W.

Since
\nu^{*}E=M^{\natural}\times_{M}E=M^{\natural}\times_{{\rm Gr}(r,V)}\varPi^{*}E
and

({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{V}=M^{\natural}\times_{{\rm Gr}(r,V)}{\mathcal{U}}^{V}
, under this identification,

\psi
induces the following homomorphism of holomorphic vector bundles on
M^{\natural}

\psi^{\natural}\colon\nu^{*}E\to({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{V}\subset({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}\varPi^{*}V,\qquad\psi^{\natural}:={\rm id}_{M^{\natural}}\times\psi.

Let
N^{\natural}\subset M^{\natural}
be the degeneracy locus of
\psi^{\natural}
, i.e., the locus where
\psi^{\natural}
is not injective.
Then
M^{\natural}\setminus N^{\natural}\supset\nu^{-1}(M^{0})
by the injective of
\psi|_{M^{0}}
.
Since
{\rm Im}\,\psi^{\natural}|_{M^{0}}\subset({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{V}|_{M^{0}}
,
we get
\psi^{\natural}(\nu^{*}E)\subset({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{V}
by the continuity of
\psi^{\natural}
.
Let
h_{{\mathcal{U}}^{V}}
be the Hermitian metric on
{\mathcal{U}}^{V}
induced from
\varPi^{*}h_{V}
via the inclusion

{\mathcal{U}}^{V}\subset\varPi^{*}V
.
Set

{E}^{\natural}:=\nu^{*}E,\qquad{W}^{\natural}:=({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{V},\qquad h_{E^{\natural}}:=\nu^{*}h_{E},\qquad h_{W^{\natural}}:=({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}h_{{\mathcal{U}}^{V}}.

We define
\overline{E^{\natural}}:=(E^{\natural},h_{E^{\natural}})
and
\overline{W^{\natural}}:=(W^{\natural},h_{W^{\natural}})
.
Then we have the following equality of differential forms on
M^{\natural}\setminus N^{\natural}

(6.13)

\widetilde{P}(\overline{E^{\natural}},\overline{W^{\natural}})=({\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}^{\natural})^{*}\widetilde{P}(\varPi^{*}\overline{E},\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{V}).

Since
\psi^{\natural}\colon\overline{E^{\natural}}\to\overline{W^{\natural}}
is a homomorphism of
holomorphic Hermitian vector bundles on
M^{\natural}
invertible on
M^{\natural}\setminus N^{\natural}
,
we have the Bott-Chern type current
\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E^{\natural}},\overline{W^{\natural}})\rangle
on
M^{\natural}

by Definition
6.1
.
By (
6.13
) and Theorem
6.2
, we have

(6.14)

\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E^{\natural}},\overline{W^{\natural}})\rangle|_{\nu^{-1}(M^{0})}=\nu^{*}\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{W})|_{M^{0}}.

Definition 6.5
.

Define the Bott-Chern type current
\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{W})\rangle
on
M
as

\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{W})\rangle:=\nu_{*}\widetilde{P}(\overline{E^{\natural}},\overline{W^{\natural}}).

By (
6.14
),
\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{W})\rangle
is an extension of

\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{W})|_{M^{0}}
to a current on
M
.

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
\chi
be a
\partial
and
\bar{\partial}
closed smooth differential from on
M
.
Let
f\colon M\to\varDelta
be a proper surjective holomorphic function with
N\subset f^{-1}(0)
. Then

f_{*}[\chi\wedge\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{W})]^{(n,n)}\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}-\left\{\int_{M^{\natural}}\nu^{*}\chi\wedge P^{M^{\natural}}_{N^{\natural}}(\psi^{\natural})\right\}\log|t|^{2}.

In particular,
\int_{M^{\natural}}\nu^{*}\chi\wedge P^{M^{\natural}}_{N^{\natural}}(\psi^{\natural})

is independent of the choice of
M^{\natural}
.

Proof.

Since

f_{*}[\chi\wedge\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E},\overline{W})\rangle]^{(n,n)}=(f\circ\nu)_{*}[\nu^{*}\chi\wedge\langle\widetilde{P}(\overline{E^{\natural}},\overline{W^{\natural}})\rangle]^{(n,n)}
,
the result follows from Theorem
6.3
.
∎

7.
The leading term of the asymptotic expansion

In this section, we consider the semi-stable reduction
f\colon Y\to T
of
\pi\colon X\to S
as in (
4.1
),
induced by the map
\mu\colon T\to S
. Recall that
F\colon Y\to X
is the corresponding morphism (cf. (
4.1
)).
Since
S
is an open subset of the compact Riemann surface
C
and
X=\pi^{-1}(S)
is the corresponding open subset of
a projective algebraic manifold
{\mathcal{X}}
(cf. Section
3.1
), we can assume the following:

•

T
is an open subset of a compact Riemann surface
C^{\prime}
.

•

Y
is an open subset of a projective algebraic manifold
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

•

f
is induced by a surjective holomorphic map from
{\mathcal{Y}}
to
C^{\prime}
.

•

F^{*}\xi
extends to a holomorphic vector bundle over
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

The goal of this section is a formula for
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
in Theorem
5.1

in terms of various characteristic classes associated to the semi-stable reduction
f\colon Y\to T
and the map
F\colon Y\to X

(Section
7.1
). As its application, we will prove a certain locality of
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
, which, together with the results in
Section
7.2
, enables us to give an explicit formula for
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
when
\xi
is topologically trivial
near
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
(Section
7.2
). In particular, when
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
is isolated, considering the two cases of
\xi
,
one trivial and the other an ample line bundle, we relate the ratio of analytic torsions with period-type integrals (Section
7.3
).

7.1.
A topological expression of the leading term

In this subsection, keeping the notation in Subsection
6.5
, we write
{\rm Gr}(n,TY)
for
{\mathbf{P}}(TY)^{\lor}
.
To simplify the notation, we denote by
T\pi
and
Tf
the relative tangent bundles of
\pi
and
f
, respectively.
Hence
T\pi=TX/S=\ker\pi_{*}|_{X\setminus\Sigma_{\pi}}
and
Tf=TY/T=\ker f_{*}|_{Y\setminus\Sigma_{f}}
.

Let

\gamma_{Y}\colon Y\setminus\Sigma_{f}\ni y\to[T_{y}Y_{f(y)}]\in{\rm Gr}(n,TY)

be the Gauss map for the family
f\colon Y\to T
. Define
M
as a resolution of the indeterminacy of
\gamma_{Y}
.
Namely, there exists a birational morphism

b\colon M\to Y

and a morphism
\gamma_{M}\colon M\to{\rm Gr}(n,TY)
such that

\gamma_{M}=\gamma_{Y}\circ b
on
M\setminus b^{-1}(\Sigma_{f})
.
We set
N:=(F\circ b)^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})\supset b^{-1}(\Sigma_{f})
and

M^{0}:=M\setminus N=b^{-1}(Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})).

Let
\varPi\colon{\rm Gr}(n,TY)\to Y
be the projection. We set

E:=\gamma_{M}^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{TY}=M\times_{{\rm Gr}(n,TY)}{\mathcal{U}}^{TY}\subset M\times_{{\rm Gr}(n,TY)}\varPi^{*}(TY),

where
{\mathcal{U}}^{TY}\subset\varPi^{*}(TY)
is the universal bundle of rank
n
on
{\rm Gr}(n,TY)
.
Let
h_{\mathcal{Y}}
be a Kähler metric on
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Then
E
is endowed with the metric induced from
\varPi^{*}h_{\mathcal{Y}}|_{Y}
. We set

V:=(F\circ b)^{*}(TX)=M\times_{{\rm Gr}(n,TY)}\varPi^{*}F^{*}(TX),

which is endowed with the Hermitian metric induced from
F^{*}(h_{\mathcal{X}}|_{X})
. Let

D_{F}\colon TY\to F^{*}(TX),\qquad\qquad J_{F}\colon Tf\to F^{*}(TX)

be the differential of the map
F\colon Y\to X
, where
J_{F}:=D_{F}|_{Tf}
.
Then
D_{F}|_{Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})}
is an isomorphism of holomorphic vector bundles from
TY
to
F^{*}(TX)

on
Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})
, and
J_{F}
is an injective homomorphism from
Tf
to
F^{*}(TX)
.
We define

\psi:={\rm id}_{M}\times\varPi^{*}D_{F}|_{\nu_{M}^{*}{\mathcal{U}}^{TY}}\in{\rm Hom}(E,V),

where

{\rm id}_{M}\times\varPi^{*}D_{F}\colon M\times_{{\rm Gr}(n,TY)}\varPi^{*}(TY)\to M\times_{{\rm Gr}(n,TY)}\varPi^{*}F^{*}(TX)

is the homomorphism induced from
D_{F}
. Since
\psi|_{M^{0}}\colon E|_{M^{0}}\to V|_{M^{0}}
is the pull back of the homomorphism

J_{F}\colon Tf\to(F^{*}TX)|_{Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})}
by
b
,

\psi\colon E\to V
is viewed as its extension. In particular,
\psi\colon E\to V
is injective on
M^{0}
.

As in Section
6.5
, we define
\nu\colon M^{\natural}\to M
as a resolution of the meromorphic map

{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}\colon M^{0}\to{\rm Gr}(n,V)
, where

{\mathbf{s}}_{\psi}(x)=[J_{F}(T_{b(x)}Y_{f(b(x))})]\in{\rm Gr}(n,T_{F(b(x))}X)\qquad(x\in M^{0}).

By constructions in Section
6.5
, we have the rank
n
holomorphic Hermitian vector bundles

\overline{E^{\natural}}
,
\overline{W^{\natural}}
on
M^{\natural}
and the homomorphism

\psi^{\natural}\colon E^{\natural}\to W^{\natural}
. We write
J_{F}^{\natural}
,
(Tf)^{\natural}
,
(F^{*}T\pi)^{\natural}
in place of

\psi^{\natural}
,
E^{\natural}
,
W^{\natural}
, respectively.
On
\nu^{-1}(M^{0})
,
J_{F}^{\natural}\colon(Tf)^{\natural}\to(F^{*}T\pi)^{\natural}
is identified with

J_{F}\colon Tf|_{Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})}\to F^{*}(T\pi)|_{Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})}
.
Hence
J_{F}^{\natural}\colon\overline{Tf^{\natural}}\to\overline{F^{*}(T\pi)^{\natural}}
is viewed as an extension of
the homomorphism of holomorphic Hermitian vector bundles

J_{F}\colon\overline{Tf}|_{Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})}\to F^{*}(\overline{T\pi})|_{Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})}
,
where
Tf
is endowed with
h_{{\mathcal{Y}}/C^{\prime}}
,
the Hermitian metric on
T{\mathcal{Y}}/C|_{{\mathcal{Y}}\setminus\Sigma_{f}}
induced from
h_{\mathcal{Y}}

and
T\pi
is endowed with
h_{{\mathcal{X}}/C}
. We make the following definitions:

(i)

{\sigma}_{J_{F}^{\natural}}\colon(M^{\natural}\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1})\setminus(N\times\{\infty\})\to{\rm Gr}(n,(Tf)^{\natural}(1)\oplus(F^{*}T\pi)^{\natural}(1))

is defined by (
6.4
), which is a meromorphic map from

M^{\natural}\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1}
to
{\rm Gr}(n,(Tf)^{\natural}(1)\oplus(F^{*}T\pi)^{\natural}(1))
.

(ii)

q^{\natural}\colon{\mathfrak{M}}^{\natural}\to M^{\natural}\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1}
is a resolution of the indeterminacy of

\sigma_{J_{F}^{\natural}}
.

(iii)

{\mathfrak{D}}_{J_{F}^{\natural}}
is the divisor defined as
(q^{\natural})^{*}(N^{\natural}\times\{\infty\})
.

(iv)

\pi_{1}^{\natural}\colon M^{\natural}\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1}\to M^{\natural}
is the projection.

(v)

\widetilde{\sigma}_{J_{F}^{\natural}}\colon{\mathfrak{M}}^{\natural}\to{\rm Gr}(n,(Tf)^{\natural}(1)\oplus(F^{*}T\pi)^{\natural}(1))

is the morphism satisfying
\widetilde{\sigma}_{J_{F}^{\natural}}={\sigma}_{J_{F}^{\natural}}\circ q^{\natural}
.

From Theorem
6.6
, we obtain the following:

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
P
be a
{\rm GL}({\mathbf{C}}^{n})
-invariant polynomial in
\mathfrak{gl}({\mathbf{C}}^{n})
with respect to the adjoint action.
Then for any
\partial
and
\bar{\partial}
closed smooth differential form
\chi
on
Y
,

f_{*}\left[\widetilde{P}(\overline{Tf},F^{*}\overline{T\pi})\wedge\chi\right]\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}-\left\{\int_{M^{\natural}}P^{M^{\natural}}_{N^{\natural}}(J_{F}^{\natural})\wedge(b\circ\nu)^{*}\chi\right\}\log|t|^{2}.

Proof.

Since the homomorphism
J_{F}^{\natural}\colon(Tf)^{\natural}\to(F^{*}T\pi)^{\natural}
on
\nu^{-1}(M^{0})
is identified with
the differential
J_{F}\colon Tf\to F^{*}(T\pi)
on
Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})
, we have

b^{*}\widetilde{P}(\overline{Tf},F^{*}(\overline{T\pi}))=\widetilde{P}(\overline{(Tf)^{\natural}},\overline{(F^{*}T\pi)^{\natural}})

as differential forms on
M^{0}
. The result then follows from Theorem
6.6
.
∎

For a square matrix
A
, we set

{\rm Td}^{\lor}(A):={\rm Td}(A)\,e^{-c_{1}(A)}=\det\left(\frac{A}{I-e^{-A}}\right)\,e^{-{\rm Tr}(A)}=\det\left(\frac{A}{e^{A}-I}\right).

Then we have
{\rm Td}^{\lor}(A)={\rm Td}(-A)
. We define a rational number
\beta_{Y/X,\xi}
by

(7.1)

\displaystyle\beta_{Y/X,\xi}

\displaystyle:=\int_{M^{\natural}}({\rm Td}^{\lor})^{M^{\natural}}_{N^{\natural}}(J_{F}^{\natural})\wedge(F\circ b\circ\nu)^{*}{\rm ch}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{J_{F}^{\natural}}}\widetilde{\sigma}_{J_{F}^{\natural}}^{*}{\rm Td}^{\lor}({\mathscr{U}}^{\natural})\wedge(F\circ b\circ\nu\circ{\pi}_{1}^{\natural}\circ{q}^{\natural})^{*}{\rm ch}(\xi).

Let
\|\cdot\|_{Q,\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}
be the Quillen metric on
\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))|_{T^{o}}
with respect to
the metrics
h_{{\mathcal{Y}}/C}
and
F^{*}h_{\xi}
.
Similarly, let
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{Q,\lambda(\mu^{*}\xi)}
be the Quillen metric on
\lambda(K_{Y/S}(F^{*}\xi))|_{T^{o}}
with respect to
the metrics
F^{*}h_{{\mathcal{X}}/C}
and
F^{*}h_{\xi}
.

Proposition 7.2
.

The following equality of functions on
T^{o}
holds:

\log\left(\frac{\|\cdot\|_{Q,\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}}{\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{Q,\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}}\right)^{2}\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}-\beta_{Y/X,\xi}\log|t|^{2}.

In particular,
\beta_{Y/X,\xi}
is independent of the choices of
M
and
M^{\natural}
.

Proof.

Since
\widetilde{{\rm Td}\cdot e^{-c_{1}}}(\overline{Tf},F^{*}(\overline{T\pi}))=\widetilde{{\rm Td}^{\lor}}(\overline{Tf},F^{*}(\overline{T\pi}))

on
Y\setminus F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})
, it follows from the anomaly formula

[
10
, Ths. 0.2, 0.3]
and
[
24
, I, Prop. 1.3.1]
that

\log\left(\frac{\|\cdot\|_{Q,\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}}{\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{Q,\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}}\right)^{2}=f_{*}\left[\widetilde{\rm Td}^{\lor}(\overline{Tf},F^{*}(\overline{T\pi}))\wedge F^{*}{\rm ch}(\overline{\xi})\right]^{(n,n)}.

This, together with Theorem
7.1
, yields the result.
∎

Recall that the rational number
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
and the non-negative integer
\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}

were defined in (
5.1
) and (
5.2
), respectively, with
g=1
.

Theorem 7.3
.

The following equality of rational numbers holds:

(7.2)

\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=\frac{1}{\deg\mu}\left(\alpha_{f,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)}+\beta_{Y/X,\xi}\right).

In particular,
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
is given by the integral of various characteristic classes associated to
the semi-stable reduction of
\pi\colon X\to S
.
Moreover, the right hand side of (
7.2
) is independent of the choice of a semi-stable reduction of
\pi\colon X\to S
.

Proof.

Let
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2},\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}
be the
L^{2}
-metric on
\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))
with respect to the metrics

h_{{\mathcal{Y}}/C^{\prime}}
and
F^{*}h_{\xi}
. Similarly, let
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{L^{2},\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}
be the
L^{2}
-metric on

\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))
with respect to the metrics
F^{*}h_{{\mathcal{X}}/C}
and
F^{*}h_{\xi}
.
Let
\tau(Y_{t},K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))
be the analytic torsion of
F^{*}\xi_{t}
with respect to the metrics
h_{\mathcal{Y}}|_{Y_{t}}
and

F^{*}h_{\xi}|_{Y_{t}}
.
On the other hand, the analytic torsion of
F^{*}\xi_{t}
with respect to the metrics
F^{*}h_{{\mathcal{X}}/C}|_{Y_{t}}
and

F^{*}h_{\xi}|_{Y_{t}}
is given by
\tau(X_{\mu(t)},K_{X_{\mu(t)}}(\xi))
. Hence

(7.3)

\log\left(\frac{\|\cdot\|_{Q,\lambda(K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))}}{\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{Q,\lambda(K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))}}\right)^{2}=\log\frac{\tau(Y_{t},K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))}{\tau(X_{\mu(t)},K_{X_{\mu(t)}}(\xi))}+\log\left(\frac{\|\cdot\|_{L^{2},\lambda(K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))}}{\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{L^{2},\lambda(K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))}}\right)^{2}.

Since
Y_{0}
is reduced and normal crossing, it follows from Theorem
5.16
that as
t\to 0
,

(7.4)

\log\tau(Y_{t},K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi_{t}))=\alpha_{f,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)}\log|t|^{2}+O\left(\log\log(|t|^{-2})\right).

Let
\sigma
be a nowhere vanishing holomorphic section of
\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))
defined on
T
.
By Proposition
5.15
, as
t\to 0
, we have

(7.5)

\log\|\sigma(t)\|_{L^{2},\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}^{2}=O\left(\log\log(|t|^{-2})\right).

Similarly, since the degenerate Kähler form
\kappa_{Y}
on
Y
is defined as
\kappa_{Y}=F^{*}\kappa_{X}+f^{*}\kappa_{T}
,
the
L^{2}
-metric on
\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))
is given by
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{L^{2},\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}
.
By Theorem
4.12
(3), as
t\to 0
, we have

(7.6)

\log\|\sigma(t)\|^{\prime 2}_{L^{2},\lambda(K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi))}=O\left(\log\log(|t|^{-2})\right).

By (
7.3
), (
7.4
), (
7.5
), (
7.6
)
and Proposition
7.2
, we get

(7.7)

\displaystyle\log\tau(X_{\mu(t)},K_{X_{\mu(t)}}(\xi))

\displaystyle=\log\tau(Y_{t},K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))-\log\left(\frac{\|\cdot\|_{Q,\lambda(K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))}}{\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{Q,\lambda(K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))}}\right)^{2}+\log\left(\frac{\|\cdot\|_{L^{2},\lambda(K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))}}{\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{L^{2},\lambda(K_{Y_{t}}(F^{*}\xi))}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{f,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)}+\beta_{Y/X,\xi})\log|t|^{2}+O\left(\log\log(|t|^{-2})\right).

Since
t=s^{\frac{1}{\deg\mu}}
, (
7.2
) follows from (
5.4
) and (
7.7
).
This completes the proof.
∎

To explore the properties of
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
, we consider its behavior when
\xi
is twisted by high tensor powers of
a positive line bundle. In the case of a fixed compact Kähler manifold (i.e., for a fixed parameter
s
),
the asymptotic behavior of analytic torsion was determined by Bismut-Vasserot
[
14
]
and Finski
[
21
]
.
(See
[
3
]
for the case where the curvature of the twisting ample line bundle is not positive.)

Corollary 7.4
.

Let
H
be a positive line bundle on
{\mathcal{X}}
and set
\xi(m):=\xi\otimes H^{m}
.
Then there exists a polynomial
p(m)\in{\mathbf{Q}}[m]
with
\deg p(m)\leq\dim{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
such that for all
m\geq 0
,

\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi(m))}=p(m).

Proof.

By (
3.8
), (
7.1
), (
7.2
),

\deg\mu\cdot\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi(m))}=\alpha_{f,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}(H^{m}\otimes\xi))}+\beta_{Y/X,H^{m}\otimes\xi}

is a polynomial in the variable
m
with rational coefficients.
Since
c_{1}(H)^{k}=0
on
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
for
k>\dim{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
, we get

\deg\alpha_{f,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}(H^{m}\otimes\xi))}\leq\dim{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
by (
3.8
) and

\deg\beta_{Y/X,H^{m}\otimes\xi}\leq\dim{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
by (
7.1
),
where
\deg(\cdot)
denotes the degree in the variable
m
.
This proves
\deg p(m)\leq\dim{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
.
∎

Example 7.5
.

Consider the same situation as in Section
5.3.4
. Hence,
\pi(z)
is locally defined by the equation
\pi(z)=z_{0}z_{1}

in a suitable local coordinates near the critical locus
\Sigma=\Sigma_{\pi}
of
\pi
. Then
\Sigma
is a compact complex submanifold
of
X
of dimension
n-1
. By Proposition
5.17
,
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi(m))}
is a polynomial in the variable
m

of degree
n-1
given by

\displaystyle\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi(m))}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}{\rm Td}(T\Sigma){\rm E}(N_{\Sigma/X}){\rm ch}(K_{\Sigma}(\xi(m)))

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{{\rm E}(0)r(\xi)}{(n-1)!}\int_{\Sigma}c_{1}(H)^{n-1}\right)m^{n-1}+O(m^{n-2})

with
{\rm E}(0)=1/6
. Suppose that
\dim\Sigma=1
and
\Sigma
is connected. Let
g
be the genus of
\Sigma
.
Since
{\rm E}(N_{\Sigma/X})
is a polynomial in
c_{2}(N_{\Sigma/X})
, we get

2\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi(m))}=-{\rm E}(0)\chi(\Sigma,K_{\Sigma}(\xi(m)))=-{\rm E}(0)\{\deg_{\Sigma}(\xi(m)|_{\Sigma})+r(\xi)(g-1)\},

where
\chi(\Sigma,K_{\Sigma}(\xi(m)))\geq 0
for
m>0
by the Nakano vanishing theorem.
In particular,
2\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}}=-{\rm E}(0)(g-1)
, whose sign is determined by
g
.
It is remarkable that
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}}=0
if
\Sigma
consists of elliptic curves.

Example 7.6
.

Suppose that
\dim{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}=0
.
In contrast to the previous example, in
[
19
]
, Eriksson and Freixas i Montplet proved
-\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}}>0

when
n=1
. In the case
n\geq 2
, they conjecture
-\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}}>0
.
See
[
19
, Conjecture, Theorems A and B]
for their conjecture and various supporting evidences.

Problem 7.7
.

In
[
3
,
14
,
21
]
, the behavior of the function
\log\tau(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi(m)))
on

S^{o}\times{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
as
m\to\infty
was studied for a fixed
s\in S^{o}
.
In Theorems
5.1
and
7.3
, the behavior as
s\to 0
is studied for a fixed
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.
In Corollary
7.4
, the behavior of
\log\tau(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi(m)))/\log|s|^{2}
as
s\to 0
is studied
for a fixed
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.
Describe the behavior of
\log\tau(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi(m)))
as a function of two variables on
S^{o}\times{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.

Recall that
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}\in{\mathbf{Q}}
was defined by (
4.32
). By (
5.7
),
the leading term of the singularity of the
L^{2}
-metric on
\lambda(K_{X/S}(\xi))
with respect to
h_{{\mathcal{X}}/C}
,

h_{\xi}
is given by
-\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
in the sense that as
s\to 0
,

\log\|\sigma(s)\|_{\lambda(K_{X/S}(\xi)),L^{2}}^{2}=-\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}\log|s|^{2}+\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c+O\left(1/\log|s|^{-1}\right).

By Theorem
7.3
,
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
is expressed as the integral of characteristic classes associated
to the semi-stable reduction of
\pi\colon X\to S
.

Corollary 7.8
.

The following identity of rational numbers holds:

\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=\frac{1}{\deg\mu}\left(\alpha_{f,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)}+\beta_{Y/X,\xi}\right)-\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}.

In particular,
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
is given by the integral of various characteristic classes associated to the semi-stable reduction
of
\pi\colon X\to S
and to the resolution of the indeterminacy of the Gauss map of
\pi
.
Consequently, there exists a polynomial
q(m)\in{\mathbf{Q}}[m]
with
\deg q(m)\leq\dim{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
such that for
m\geq 0
,

\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi(m))}=q(m).

Proof.

By (
5.1
) and (
5.4
), we have

\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}+\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
.
Comparing this with (
7.2
), we get the first statement.
The second statement follows from the same argument as in the proof of Corollary
7.4
.
∎

Concerning the subdominant term of the asymptotic expansion (
5.4
) with
g=1
,

\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(m)}
is independent of the choice of an ample line bundle
H
on
X
and
m>0
under the assumption
that
n=1
and
X_{0}
is reduced. In fact, in this case,
\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(m)}-\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}}=N-1
,
where
N
is the number of the irreducible components of
X_{0}
by
[
15
, Th. 5.5]
.
In higher dimensions, we propose the following:

Conjecture 7.9
.

If
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
is isolated,
\xi={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
and
n>1
, then
\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(m)}
is independent of the choice of
an ample line bundle
H
and
m>0
. Moreover,

\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(m)}-\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}}=\dim\ker\square_{X_{0}}^{(0,n-1)}-h^{0,n-1}(X_{s}),\qquad s\not=0.

Here
\square_{X_{0}}^{(0,n-1)}
is the Friedrichs extension of the Hodge-Kodaira Laplacian acting on the compactly supported
smooth
(0,n-1)
-forms on
X_{0}\setminus{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
.

7.2.
A locality of the leading term

In this subsection, we prove the locality of the leading term in the asymptotic expansion of analytic torsion.
Let
\psi\colon{\mathcal{W}}\to B
be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers from a connected projective manifold
of dimension
n+1
to a compact Riemann surface. Let
\Sigma_{\psi}\subset{\mathcal{W}}
be the critical locus of
\psi
.
Let
b_{0}\in\psi(\Sigma_{\psi})\subset B
be a point of the discriminant locus of
\psi
. We assume that there is an embedding

(S,0)\hookrightarrow(B,b_{0})
and we set
W:=\psi^{-1}(S)
. For
s\in S
, we set
W_{s}:=\psi^{-1}(s)
.
Let
(\eta,h_{\eta})
be a holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle on
{\mathcal{W}}
such that
(\eta,h_{\eta})|_{W}
is Nakano semi-positive.
Let
h_{W}
be a Kähler metric on
W
and let
h_{W_{s}}
be the Kähler metric on
W_{s}
induced from
h_{W}
.
Let
\tau(W_{s},K_{W_{s}}(\eta_{s}))
be the analytic torsion of
(W_{s},K_{W_{s}}(\eta_{s}))
with respect to the metrics
h_{W_{s}}
,

h_{\eta}|_{W_{s}}
.

Theorem 7.10
.

With the notation and assumption as above, let
U
be an open neighborhood of
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
in
{\mathcal{X}}
and
let
V
be an open neighborhood of
{\rm Sing}\,W_{0}
in
{\mathcal{W}}
. Suppose that the following hold:

(1)

There exists an isomorphism
\iota\colon U\to V

such that
\iota^{*}(\psi|_{V})=\pi|_{U}
and
\iota^{*}(\eta|_{V})\cong\xi|_{U}
as smooth complex vector bundles on
U
.

(2)

There exist log-resolutions
b\colon(X^{\prime},X^{\prime}_{0})\to(X,X_{0})
and
c\colon(W^{\prime},W^{\prime}_{0})\to(W,W_{0})
such that the isomorphism

\iota\colon U\to V
lifts to an isomorphism between
b^{-1}(U)
and
c^{-1}(V)
,
such that
X^{\prime}\setminus X^{\prime}_{0}\subset X^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime}\setminus W^{\prime}_{0}\subset W^{\prime}
are toroidal embeddings without self-intersection,
and such that the conical polyhedral complexes with integral structure associated to

X^{\prime}\setminus X^{\prime}_{0}\subset X^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime}\setminus W^{\prime}_{0}\subset W^{\prime}
coincide.
(See
[
28
, p.71]
for the notion of the conical polyhedral complex with integral structure associated to a toroidal embedding
without self-intersection.)

Then
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=\kappa_{\psi,K_{W/S}(\eta)}
.
In particular, there exist
\varrho_{\xi,\eta}\in{\mathbf{Z}}
and
c_{\xi,\eta}\in{\mathbf{R}}
such that as
s\to 0
,

\log\frac{\tau(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(\xi_{s}))}{\tau(W_{s},K_{W_{s}}(\eta_{s}))}=\varrho_{\xi,\eta}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c_{\xi,\eta}+O(1/\log|s|^{-1}).

Proof.

Since
\iota^{*}(\psi|_{V})=\pi|_{U}
by (1) and the conical polyhedral complexes with integral structure associated to

X^{\prime}\setminus X^{\prime}_{0}\subset X^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime}\setminus W^{\prime}_{0}\subset W^{\prime}
are isomorphic by (2),
we obtain a semi-stable reduction of
\psi\colon(W,W_{0})\to(S,0)
by the same procedure
as the construction of the semi-stable reduction
f\colon Y\to T
of
\pi\colon X\to S
(
[
28
, Chap. II Sect. 3]
).
The semi-stable reduction of
\psi\colon(W,W_{0})\to(S,0)
constructed in this way is denoted by
g\colon M\to T
.
Let
G\colon M\to W
be the corresponding morphism.
Since
\iota^{*}(\eta|_{V})\cong\xi|_{U}
as smooth vector bundles on a neighborhood of
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
,
we obtain
\alpha_{f,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)}=\alpha_{g,K_{M/T}(G^{*}\eta)}
and
\beta_{Y/X,\xi}=\beta_{M/W,\eta}
by
(
3.8
) and (
7.1
), respectively. The result follows from Theorem
7.3
.
∎

Remark 7.11
.

If
X_{0}
and
W_{0}
are irreducible, (2) can be dropped in Theorem
7.10
.

Recall that the semi-simple part of the monodromy operator
M_{s}
acts on the limiting Hodge filtration
F^{n}_{\infty}H^{n+q}(X_{\infty})

and that
\log M_{s}
is its logarithm with imaginary part lying in
[0,2\pi)
. To simplify the notation, we set

(7.8)

\epsilon_{\pi}:=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{q\geq 0}(-1)^{q}{\rm Tr}\left[\left.\log M_{s}\right|_{F^{n}_{\infty}H^{n+q}(X_{\infty})}\right].

By Proposition
5.7
, we have

(7.9)

\epsilon_{\pi}=\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}}.

When
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
consists of isolated points, recall that
\mu(\pi_{x})
and
\widetilde{p}_{g}(\pi_{x})
,
x\in{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
,
denote the Milnor number and the spectral genus of
\pi_{x}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
, respectively.

Theorem 7.12
.

Let
(\xi,h_{\xi})
be a holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle of rank
r(\xi)
on
{\mathcal{X}}
such that

(\xi,h_{\xi})|_{X}
is Nakano semi-positive. Then the following hold.

(1)

If
\xi
is topologically trivial near
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
, then

\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=r(\xi)(\alpha_{\pi,K_{X/S}}+\epsilon_{\pi}).

(2)

If
\dim{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}=0
, then

\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=-r(\xi)\sum_{x\in{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}}\left(\frac{\mu(\pi_{x})}{(n+2)!}-\widetilde{p}_{g}(\pi_{x})\right).

Proof.

Setting
{\mathcal{W}}={\mathcal{X}}
and
\eta={\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{r(\xi)}
in Theorem
7.10
,
we obtain (1) from Corollary
5.8
and (2) from Proposition
5.9
.
∎

The locality of
\kappa_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
entails the corresponding locality of
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}
.

Theorem 7.13
.

With the same notation and assumption as in Theorem
7.10
,
one has
\delta_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}=\delta_{\psi,K_{W/S}(\eta)}
. In particular,
if
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are nowhere vanishing holomorphic sections on
S
of
\lambda(K_{X/S}(\xi))
and

\lambda(K_{W/S}(\eta))
respectively, then there exists
c^{\prime}_{\xi,\eta}\in{\mathbf{R}}
such that as
s\to 0
,

\log(\|\sigma(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}/\|\sigma^{\prime}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2})=-\varrho_{\xi,\eta}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c^{\prime}_{\xi,\eta}+O(1/\log|s|^{-1}),

where
\varrho_{\xi,\eta}\in{\mathbf{Z}}
is the same integer as in Theorem
7.10
.

Proof.

The second statement follows from Theorems
3.4
and
7.10
.
The first statement follows from the second one.
∎

Theorem 7.14
.

With the same notation and assumption as in Theorem
7.12
,
let
\sigma
be a nowhere vanishing holomorphic section of
\lambda(K_{X/S}(\xi))
defined on
S
.

(1)

If
\xi
is topologically trivial near
{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}
, then there exists
c^{\prime}_{\xi}\in{\mathbf{R}}
such that as
s\to 0
,

\log\|\sigma(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-r(\xi)\epsilon_{\pi}\log|s|^{2}+\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c^{\prime}_{\xi}+O(1/\log|s|^{-1}).

(2)

If
\dim{\rm Sing}\,X_{0}=0
, then there exists
c^{\prime}_{\xi}\in{\mathbf{R}}
such that as
s\to 0
,

\log\|\sigma(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-r(\xi)\widetilde{p}_{g}({\rm Sing}\,X_{0})\log|s|^{2}+\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(\xi)}\log\log(|s|^{-2})+c^{\prime}_{\xi}+O(1/\log|s|^{-1}).

Proof.

The result follows from Theorems
3.4
and
7.12
.
∎

Question 7.15
.

In the situation of Theorems
7.10
and
7.14
,
do the elementary exponents of
R^{q}\psi_{*}K_{W/S}(\eta)
coincide with those of
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)
for all
q\geq 0
?

7.3.
Analytic torsion and the determinant of the period-type integrals

In this subsection, as an application of Theorems
7.12
(2) and
7.14
(2),
we relate the ratio of analytic torsions to the ratio of the determinants of period-type integrals.

Theorem 7.16
.

Let
H
be an ample line bundle on
{\mathcal{X}}
endowed with a Hermitian metric of semi-positive curvature.
Let
\{\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m_{1}}\}
,
m_{1}=h^{0}(K_{X_{s}}(H))
, be a basis of
\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(H)

as a free
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-module.
Similarly, let
\{\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{m_{2}}\}
,
m_{2}=h^{n,0}(X_{s})
, be a basis of
\pi_{*}K_{X/S}

as a free
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-module.
Suppose that
X_{0}
has only isolated singularities. Then there exist constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
such that as
s\to 0
,

(7.10)

\frac{\tau(X_{s},K_{X_{s}})}{\tau(X_{s},K_{X_{s}}(H))}\sim C\frac{\displaystyle\det\left((\sqrt{-1})^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{s}}h_{H}(\varphi_{\alpha}(s)\wedge\overline{\varphi_{\beta}(s)})\right)}{\displaystyle\det\left((\sqrt{-1})^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{s}}\omega_{i}(s)\wedge\overline{\omega_{j}(s)}\right)}\sim C^{\prime}(\log|s|^{-1})^{\varrho},

where
\varrho:=\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(H)}-\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}}
and
A(s)\sim B(s)
if
\lim_{s\to 0}A(s)/B(s)=1
.
Moreover, if the linear system
|H|
contains a smooth member avoiding
\Sigma_{\pi}
and intersecting
X_{0}
transversally,
then
\varrho\geq 0
.

Proof.

We keep the notation of Theorems
7.12
and
7.14
. Set
\xi={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
and
\xi^{\prime}=H
.
Since
H
is ample,
\sigma^{\prime}=\varphi_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\varphi_{m_{2}}
by the Kodaira vanishing theorem. Hence

(7.11)

\|\sigma^{\prime}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\det\left((\sqrt{-1})^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{s}}h_{H}(\varphi_{\alpha}(s)\wedge\overline{\varphi_{\beta}(s)})\right)\qquad(s\in S^{o}).

Since
X_{0}
has only isolated singularities, the monodromy action on
H^{q}(X_{s},{\mathbf{C}})

(s\in S^{o})
is trivial for
0\leq q<n
.
Since
(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S},h_{L^{2}})
is the dual of the Hermitian vector bundle
(R^{n-q}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X},h_{L^{2}})

by the Serre duality, we deduce from the triviality of the monodromy action on
H^{q}(X_{s},{\mathbf{C}})

(0\leq q<n)

and
[
18
, Th. 4.4]
that the
L^{2}
-metric on
\det R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}
defined on
S^{o}
extends to
a non-degenerate continuous metric on
\det R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}
defined on
S
for
q>0
.
Hence there exists a continuous function
g(s)
on
S
such that

(7.12)

\|\sigma(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=e^{g(s)}\det\left((\sqrt{-1})^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{s}}\omega_{i}(s)\wedge\overline{\omega_{j}(s)}\right)\qquad(s\in S^{o}).

From Theorems
7.12
(2) and
7.14
and (
7.11
), (
7.12
),
we obtain (
7.10
).

We prove the non-negativity of
\varrho
. Let
D\in|H|
be a smooth hypersurface in
X
with
D\cap\Sigma_{\pi}=\emptyset

and intersecting
X_{0}
transversally.
Let
f\colon Y\to T
be the semi-stable reduction of
\pi\colon X\to S
as in (
4.1
).
Then
F^{-1}(D)
is a smooth hypersurface in
Y
with
F^{-1}(D)\cap F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})=\emptyset
and
intersecting
Y_{0}
transversally.
Let
\{\widetilde{\varphi}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\varphi}_{m_{1}}\}
and
\{\widetilde{\omega}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\omega}_{m_{2}}\}

be bases of
f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}H)
and
f_{*}K_{Y/T}
as free
{\mathcal{O}}_{T}
-modules, respectively.
To see that we can assume

\{\widetilde{\omega}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\omega}_{m_{2}}\}\subset\{\widetilde{\varphi}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\varphi}_{m_{1}}\}
,
consider the exact sequence
0\to f_{*}K_{Y/T}\to f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}H)\to f_{*}(K_{D/T})

obtained from the Poincaré residue sequence for
F^{-1}(D)\subset Y
.
Since
f_{*}K_{Y/T}
,
f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}H)
, and
f_{*}(K_{D/T})
are free
{\mathcal{O}}_{T}
-modules,

f_{*}K_{Y/T}
is a direct summand of
f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}H)
. Consequently, we can assume

\{\widetilde{\omega}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\omega}_{m_{2}}\}\subset\{\widetilde{\varphi}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\varphi}_{m_{1}}\}
.

By Theorem
4.12
(3), we have

(7.13)

\varrho_{f,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}H)}=\varrho_{f,K_{X/S}(H)},\qquad\varrho_{f,K_{Y/T}}=\varrho_{f,K_{X/S}}.

By
[
45
, Lemma 5.2]
, there is a basis
\{\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{m_{1}}\}
of
f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}H)
satisfying

{\rm ord}(\|\psi_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\psi_{m_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})={\rm ord}(\prod_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\|\psi_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})

and
{\rm ord}(\|\psi_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\geq({\rm ord}\|\psi_{l}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\geq 0
whenever
k<l
.
Here for
f(t)=\sum_{l=0}^{n}a_{l}(t)(-\log|t|^{2})^{l}
with
a_{l}(t)\in C^{\infty}(T)
and
(a_{0}(0),\ldots,a_{n}(0))\not=(0,\ldots,0)
,
we set
{\rm ord}(f):=\max\{0\leq l\leq n;\,a_{l}(0)\not=0\}
. Then

(7.14)

\varrho_{f,K_{Y/T}(F^{*}H)}=\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}{\rm ord}(\|\psi_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

Express
\omega_{k}=\sum_{l=1}^{m_{1}}c_{kl}(t)\psi_{l}
,
1\leq k\leq m_{1}
,
where the functions
c_{kl}(t)\in{\mathcal{O}}(T)
satisfy
{\rm rank}(c_{kl}(0))=m_{2}
.
We can find a basis
\{\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{m_{2}}\}
of
f_{*}K_{Y/T}

satisfying
\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{k}\equiv\psi_{i_{k}}+\sum_{j>i_{k}}b_{i_{k},j}\psi_{j}\mod t{\mathcal{O}}(T)

and
i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{m_{2}}
, where the
b_{i_{k},j}
are constants.
Since
{\rm ord}(\|\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\leq{\rm ord}(\|\psi_{i_{k}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})

and
\|\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{m_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\prod_{k=1}^{m_{2}}\|\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
,
we obtain

(7.15)

{\rm ord}(\|\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{m_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m_{2}}{\rm ord}(\|\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m_{2}}{\rm ord}(\|\psi_{i_{k}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\leq\sum_{l=1}^{m_{1}}{\rm ord}(\|\psi_{l}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

Let
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{L^{2}}
be the
L^{2}
-norm on
f_{*}K_{Y/T}
. By
[
45
, Lemma 5.2]
again, we can assume

(7.16)

\varrho_{f,K_{Y/T}}={\rm ord}(\|\widetilde{\omega}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widetilde{\omega}_{m_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\prime 2})=\sum_{k=1}^{m_{2}}{\rm ord}(\|\widetilde{\omega}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{\prime 2}).

Since
{\rm Sing}\,Y_{0}\subset F^{-1}(\Sigma_{\pi})
and hence
F^{-1}(D)\cap{\rm Sing}\,Y_{0}=\emptyset
,
it follows from
[
45
, Lemma 3.4]
that

(7.17)

{\rm ord}(\|\widetilde{\omega}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{\prime 2})={\rm ord}(\|\widetilde{\omega}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

By (
7.13
), (
7.14
), (
7.16
), (
7.16
), (
7.17
),
we conclue
\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}}\leq\varrho_{\pi,K_{X/S}(H)}
.
∎

Remark 7.17
.

We note that the second equality of
[
15
, Conjecture 9.6]
follows from (
7.10
) and
Theorems
4.12
(3) and
7.14
(2). Conjecture
7.9
says that
\varrho
is independent of
H
.
For a conjectural interpretation of the left hand side of (
7.10
) in terms of the small eigenvalues of
the Hodge-Kodaira Laplacian of
X_{s}
, see
[
15
, Conjecture 9.6 ]
.

8.
The boundary behavior of the curvature of
L^{2}
-metrics

We keep the notation and assumption in Section
4
.
Let
{\mathcal{R}}(s)\,ds\wedge d\bar{s}
be the curvature form of
(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi),h_{L^{2}})
.
By Berndtsson
[
4
]
, Mourougane-Takayama
[
35
]
,
i{\mathcal{R}}(s)
is semi-positive.
In this section, as a by-product of Theorem
4.12
, we determine the asymptotic behavior of the first Chern form

c_{1}(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi),h_{L^{2}})
as
s\to 0
. In particular, we prove the Poincaré boundedness of the curvature

i{\mathcal{R}}(s)
.
In what follows, we write
C^{\infty}_{\bf R}(T)
for the set of
real-valued

C^{\infty}
functions on
T
.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
I
be a finite set. For
i\in I
, let
\ell_{i}\in{\mathbf{Z}}
and
\varphi_{i}(t)\in C^{\infty}_{\mathbf{R}}(T)
.
Set
g_{i}(t):=(\log|t|^{2})^{\ell_{i}}\varphi_{i}(t)
for
i\in I
and
g(t):=\sum_{i\in I}g_{i}(t)
. Suppose that
g(t)>0
on
T^{o}
.
Then the following identity of functions on
T^{o}
hold:

(8.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t\bar{t}}\log g

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i}\frac{\ell_{i}}{|t|^{2}(\log|t|^{2})^{2}}\frac{g_{i}}{g}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j}\left|\frac{\ell_{i}-\ell_{j}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}\right|^{2}\frac{g_{i}g_{j}}{g^{2}}+\sum_{i}\frac{\partial_{t\bar{t}}\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}\frac{g_{i}}{g}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i}\left|\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}\frac{g_{i}}{g}\right|^{2}+\sum_{i,j}{\rm Re}\left\{\left(\frac{\ell_{i}-\ell_{j}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}\right)\cdot\left(\overline{\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}-\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{j}}{\varphi_{j}}}\right)\right\}\frac{g_{i}g_{j}}{g^{2}}.

In particular, if
0\leq\ell_{i}\leq N
for all
i\in I
, then as
t\to 0

(8.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t\bar{t}}\log g

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i}\frac{\ell_{i}}{|t|^{2}(\log|t|^{2})^{2}}\frac{g_{i}}{g}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j}\left|\frac{\ell_{i}-\ell_{j}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}\right|^{2}\frac{g_{i}g_{j}}{g^{2}}

\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{(\log|t|^{-1})^{2N}}{|t|g(t)^{2}}+\frac{(\log|t|^{-1})^{N}}{g(t)}\right).

Proof.

Since
\partial_{t}g_{i}=\left(\frac{\ell_{i}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}+\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}\right)g_{i}
, we get

(8.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t\bar{t}}g_{i}(t)

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{\ell_{i}}{|t|^{2}(\log|t|^{2})^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{t\bar{t}}\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}-\frac{|\partial_{t}\varphi_{i}|^{2}}{\varphi_{i}^{2}}+\left|\frac{\ell_{i}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}+\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}\right|^{2}\right)g_{i}

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{\ell_{i}}{|t|^{2}(\log|t|^{2})^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{t\bar{t}}\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}+\left|\frac{\ell_{i}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}\right|^{2}+2{\rm Re}\left(\frac{\ell_{i}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}\right)\overline{\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}}\right)g_{i}.

Since

(8.4)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}g|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j\in I}\left(\frac{\ell_{i}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}+\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}\right)\left(\overline{\frac{\ell_{j}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}+\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{j}}{\varphi_{j}}}\right)g_{i}g_{j}

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i}\left(\frac{\ell_{i}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}\right)g_{i}\right|^{2}+2\sum_{i,j}{\rm Re}\left\{\left(\frac{\ell_{i}}{t(\log|t|^{2})}\right)\overline{\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{j}}{\varphi_{j}}}\right\}g_{i}g_{j}+\left|\sum_{j}\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi_{j}}{\varphi_{j}}g_{j}\right|^{2}

and
\partial_{t\bar{t}}\log g=\partial_{t\bar{t}}g/g-|\partial_{t}g|^{2}/g^{2}
,
we obtain (
8.1
) from (
8.3
), (
8.4
).
Since
\partial_{t}\varphi=O(1)
,
\partial_{t\bar{t}}\varphi=O(1)
and
g_{i}/\varphi_{i}=O((\log|t|^{-1})^{N})
,
(
8.2
) follows from (
8.1
).
∎

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
\varphi_{i}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathbf{R}}(T)

(0\leq i\leq N)
and set
g(t)=\sum_{i=0}^{N}(\log|t|^{2})^{i}\varphi_{i}(t)
.
Suppose that
g(t)>0
on
T^{o}
and
\varphi_{i}(0)\not=0
for some
0\leq i\leq N
. Set

\ell:=\max\{0\leq i\leq N;\,\varphi_{i}(0)\not=0\}
.
Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that on
T^{o}
,

\left|\partial_{t\bar{t}}\log g(t)+\frac{\ell}{|t|^{2}(\log|t|^{-1})^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{C}{|t|^{2}(\log|t|^{-1})^{3}}.

Proof.

Set
I=\{0,1,\ldots,N\}
and
g_{i}(t):=(\log|t|^{2})^{i}\varphi_{i}(t)
for
i\in I
.
Since
g(t)=\varphi_{\ell}(0)(\log|t|^{2})^{\ell}(1+O(1/\log|t|^{-1}))
as
t\to 0
, it follows from Lemma
8.1
that

(8.5)

\left|\frac{g_{i}(t)}{g(t)}\right|=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{ll}O(|t|(\log|t|^{-1})^{i-\ell})&(i>\ell),\\
1+O((\log|t|^{-1})^{-1})&(i=\ell),\\
O((\log|t|^{-1})^{-(\ell-i)})&(i<\ell).\end{array}\end{cases}

Since
O(1/g(t))=O(1)
by the formula
g(t)=\varphi_{\ell}(0)(\log|t|^{2})^{\ell}(1+O(1/\log|t|^{-1}))
,
it follows from (
8.2
) that

(8.6)

\displaystyle\partial_{t\bar{t}}\log g

\displaystyle=-\frac{\ell}{|t|^{2}(\log|t|^{2})^{2}}\frac{g_{\ell}}{g}-\sum_{i\not=\ell}\frac{i}{|t|^{2}(\log|t|^{2})^{2}}\frac{g_{i}}{g}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\not=j}\frac{(i-j)^{2}}{|t|^{2}(\log|t|^{2})^{2}}\left(\frac{g_{i}}{g}\right)\left(\frac{g_{j}}{g}\right)+O\left(\frac{(-\log|t|)^{2N}}{|t|}\right).

By (
8.5
), the second and third terms on the right-hand side of
(
8.6
) are bounded by
|t|^{-2}(-\log|t|)^{-3}
as
t\to 0
, which implies the result.
∎

Theorem 8.3
.

As
s\to 0
, the following equality holds:

(8.7)

c_{1}(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi),h_{L^{2}})=\left\{\frac{\varrho^{q}}{|s|^{2}(\log|s|)^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{|s|^{2}(\log|s|)^{3}}\right)\right\}i\,ds\wedge d\bar{s}.

In particular, the curvature form
i{\mathcal{R}}(s)\,ds\wedge d\bar{s}
is Poincaré bounded near
0\in S
.
Namely, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
s\in S^{o}
,

0\leq i{\mathcal{R}}(s)\leq\frac{C}{|s|^{2}(\log|s|)^{2}}\,{\rm Id}_{R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)}.

Proof.

By (
4.12
),

\mu^{*}c_{1}(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi),h_{L^{2}})=-\frac{i}{2\pi}\partial\bar{\partial}\log\{\sum_{m=0}^{nh^{q}}a_{m}(t)\,(\log|t|^{2})^{m}\}
.
In Lemma
8.2
, set
g(t)=\det H(t)=\sum_{m=0}^{nh^{q}}(\log|t|^{2})^{m}a_{m}(t)
.
Since
a_{m}(0)\not=0
for some
0\leq m\leq nh^{q}
, we deduce from Lemma
8.2
that

(8.8)

\mu^{*}c_{1}(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi),h_{L^{2}})=\varrho^{q}\frac{i\,dt\wedge d\bar{t}}{|t|^{2}(-\log|t|)^{2}}+O\left(\frac{i\,dt\wedge d\bar{t}}{|t|^{2}(-\log|t|)^{3}}\right).

Since
\mu^{*}\{ds\wedge d\bar{s}/(|s|^{2}(-\log|s|)^{m}\}=(\deg\mu)^{2-m}dt\wedge d\bar{t}/(|t|^{2}(-\log|t|)^{m})
,
(
8.7
) follows from (
8.8
).

Let
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{h^{q}}
be the eigenvalues of
i{\mathcal{R}}(s)
.
Since
(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi),h_{L^{2}})
is Nakano semi-positive
[
4
,
35
]
,
we have
\lambda_{\alpha}\geq 0
for all
1\leq\alpha\leq h^{q}
. This, together with (
8.8
), yields that

0\leq i\,{\rm Tr}\,{\mathcal{R}}(s)=\sum_{\alpha}\lambda_{\alpha}\leq C/(|s|^{2}(\log|s|^{-2})^{2})
on
S^{o}
.
Consequently,
\max_{\alpha}\{\lambda_{\alpha}\}\leq C/(|s|^{2}(-\log|s|)^{2})
.
Since
0\leq i{\mathcal{R}}(s)\leq\max_{\alpha}\{\lambda_{\alpha}\}\cdot{\rm Id}_{R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)}
,
the desired inequality follows from this inequality .
∎

Corollary 8.4
.

If
X_{0}
has only canonical singularities,
the pseudo distance on
S
induced by the semi-positive
(1,1)
-form
c_{1}(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi),h_{L^{2}})
is incomplete.

Proof.

Since
\varrho^{q}=0
by Corollary
5.13
, the assertion follows from Theorem
8.3
.
∎

When
\xi={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
and
q=0
, Corollary
8.4
was obtained by Wang
[
47
, Th. 1.1]
.

Remark 8.5
.

A holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle on a punctured disc is referred to as an
acceptable bundle
in the sense of Mochizuki

[
34
, Sect. 21]
if the pointwise length of its curvature form with respect to the Poincaré metric on the punctured disc
is bounded near the puncture. By Theorem
8.3
,
(R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi),h_{L^{2}})
is an acceptable bundle
on
S^{o}
in the sense of Mochizuki. By Theorem
4.12
(1), the elementary exponents of
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)

coincide with its parabolic weights.
(See e.g.
[
34
, Sect. 21.4.1]
,
[
22
, 7.11]
for the notion of parabolic weights.)
We refer the reader to
[
34
, Sect. 21]
,
[
22
]

and the references therein for further details about acceptable bundles.
It is worth remarking that a weak form of Theorem
4.12
(1)
holds for general acceptable bundles
[
34
, Th. 21.3.2]
,
[
22
, Th. 1.13]
.

By Takayama
[
45
]
, the
L^{2}
-metric
\mu^{*}h_{L^{2}}
on
f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)
is good in the sense of Mumford.
For the higher direct image sheaves, we have the following:

Proposition 8.6
.

(1)

The Hermitian metric
\mu^{*}\det h_{L^{2}}
on
\det R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)|_{T^{o}}
is good in the sense of Mumford.

(2)

If
X_{0}
is reduced and has only canonical singularities, then the
L^{2}
-metric
h_{L^{2}}
on
R^{q}\pi_{*}K_{X/S}(\xi)

is good in the sense of Mumford.

Proof.

Combining Theorem
4.12
(1), (2) and
[
45
, Prop. 4.5]
, we get (1).
Since
h_{L^{2}}
satisfies a coercivity property of the diagonal part in the sense of Takayama
[
45
, Def. 4.4]

by Theorem
5.12
, we get (2) by
[
45
, Prop. 4.5]
.
∎

Conjecture 8.7
.

For any
q>0
, the Hermitian metric
\mu^{*}h_{L^{2}}
on
R^{q}f_{*}K_{Y/T}(F^{*}\xi)
is good in the sense of Mumford.

9.
Appendix

9.1.
A sequence of Kähler forms on
Z
approximating
\kappa_{Z}

In Subsections
9.1
and
9.2
, we keep the notations in Section
4
.
We construct the Kähler forms
\{\kappa_{Z,k}\}
in the proof of Proposition
4.2
.
Although it is standard, we give a construction here for a use in the next subsection.
Since
X^{\prime}\times_{S}T
is a hypersurface in the complex manifold
X^{\prime}\times T
and
since
\widetilde{\rho}\colon Z\to X^{\prime}\times_{S}T
is a composite of blowing-ups with non-singular centers, there is a sequence

\begin{matrix}\,&\,E^{(m)}&\,&E^{(m-1)}&\,&E^{(1)}&\,&\,\\
\,&\cap&\,&\cap&\,&\cap&\,&\,\\
W&=W^{(m)}&\to&W^{(m-1)}&\to\cdots\to&W^{(1)}&\to&X^{\prime}\times T\\
\cup&\cup&\,&\cup&\,&\cup&\,&\cup\\
Z&=Z^{(m)}&\to&Z^{(m-1)}&\to\cdots\to&Z^{(1)}&\to&X^{\prime}\times_{S}T\end{matrix}

where
W^{(i)}
is a Kähler manifold,
\pi_{i}\colon W^{(i)}\to W^{(i-1)}
is a blowing-up with non-singular center

C^{(i-1)}\subset W^{(i-1)}
,
C^{(i-1)}\subset{\rm Sing}\,Z^{(i-1)}
,
Z^{(i)}
is the proper transform of
Z^{(i-1)}
by
\pi_{i}
,
and
E^{(i)}
is the set of exceptional hypersurfaces in
W^{(i)}
, i.e.,
E^{(i)}=\widetilde{E}^{(i-1)}\cup\pi_{i}^{-1}(C^{(i-1)})
,
where
\widetilde{E}^{(i-1)}
consists of the proper transforms of the hypersurfaces in
E^{(i-1)}
.
We set
\varPi_{i}:=\pi_{i+1}\circ\cdots\circ\pi_{m}
(
0\leq i\leq m-1
). Then
\widetilde{\rho}=\varPi_{0}|_{Z}
.
Recall that, by (
4.2
),
\varpi\colon Z\to T
is the projection defined as
\varpi={\rm pr}_{2}\circ\widetilde{\rho}
.
We fix a
C^{0}
-norm
\|\cdot\|
on the space of differential forms on
W^{(i)}
for all
i
.

We set
W^{(0)}:=X^{\prime}\times T
and
Z^{(0)}:=X^{\prime}\times_{S}T
. Let
\widetilde{\kappa}_{X^{\prime}}
be a Kähler form on
X^{\prime}
and
define
\omega_{W^{(0)}}:=\widetilde{\kappa}_{X^{\prime}}+\kappa_{T}
.
Let
{\mathcal{V}}^{(i)}_{l}\subset W^{(i)}
be a small open neighborhood of
\pi_{i}^{-1}(C^{(i-1)})
such that

{\rm pr}_{2}\circ\pi_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\pi_{i}({\mathcal{V}}^{(i)}_{l})\subset T(\frac{1}{l})
.
Suppose that a Kähler form
\omega_{W^{(i-1)}}^{(l)}
on
W^{(i-1)}
is given.
Let
\sigma_{i}
be the canonical section of
{\mathcal{O}}_{W^{(i)}}(\pi_{i}^{-1}(C^{(i-1)}))
with
{\rm div}(\sigma_{i})=\pi_{i}^{-1}(C^{(i-1)})
.
By
[
16
, Chap.VII, Prop. 12.4]
, there is a Hermitian metric
h^{(i)}_{l}
on
{\mathcal{O}}_{W^{(i)}}(\pi_{i}^{-1}(C^{(i-1)}))

such that
\|\sigma_{i}\|_{h^{(i)}_{l}}=1
on
W^{(i)}\setminus{\mathcal{V}}^{(i)}_{l}
and such that

\pi_{i}^{*}\omega_{W^{(i-1)}}^{(l)}+\epsilon dd^{c}\log\|\sigma_{i}\|_{h^{(i)}_{l}}^{2}
is a Kähler form on
W^{(i)}

for all
0<\epsilon\ll 1
.
Taking
\epsilon_{l}>0
small, we may suppose that
\epsilon_{l}\|dd^{c}\log\|\sigma_{i}\|_{h^{(i)}_{l}}\|<2^{-l}/m
.
Then we set
{\omega}_{W^{(i)}}^{(l)}:=\pi_{i}^{*}\omega_{W^{(i-1)}}^{(l)}+\epsilon_{l}dd^{c}\log\|\sigma_{i}\|_{h^{(i)}_{l}}^{2}
.
By construction,
{\omega}_{W^{(i)}}^{(l)}=\pi_{i}^{*}\omega_{W^{(i-1)}}^{(l)}
on
W^{(i)}\setminus{\mathcal{V}}^{(i)}_{l}
and

[{\omega}_{W^{(i)}}^{(l)}]=\pi_{i}^{*}[\omega_{W^{(i-1)}}^{(l)}]+\epsilon_{l}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{W^{(i)}}(-\pi_{i}^{-1}(C^{(i-1)})))
.
Starting with
\omega_{W^{(0)}}^{(l)}:=\omega_{W^{(0)}}
,
we obtain a sequence of Kähler manifolds
\{(W^{(i)},{\omega}_{W^{(i)}}^{(l)})\}
. We set

(9.1)

\widetilde{\kappa}_{Z,l}:={\omega}_{W}^{(l)}|_{Z}=\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\widetilde{\kappa}_{X^{\prime}}+\kappa_{T})+dd^{c}\Psi^{(l)},

where
\Psi^{(l)}:=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\epsilon_{l}\varPi_{i}^{*}\log\|\sigma_{i}\|_{h^{(i)}_{l}}^{2}|_{Z}
with
\|dd^{c}\Psi^{(l)}\|<2^{-l}
.

Let
({\mathcal{U}},(z_{0},\ldots,z_{n}))
be a coordinate neighborhood of
Z
such that

\varpi(z)=z_{0}^{e_{0}}\cdots z_{n}^{e_{n}}
, where
e_{i}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.
Since
{\rm Supp}(\varPi_{i}^{*}\sigma_{i}|_{Z})\subset Z_{0}=\varpi^{-1}(0)
, we can express

\varPi_{i}^{*}\sigma_{i}|_{\mathcal{U}}=z_{0}^{d_{0}}\cdots z_{n}^{d_{n}}{\mathbf{s}}
,
d_{i}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
,
where
{\mathbf{s}}
is a local frame of
\varPi_{i}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{W^{(i)}}(\pi_{i}^{-1}(C^{(i-1)}))|_{Z}
on
{\mathcal{U}}
.
Notice that
e_{i}=0
implies
d_{i}=0
. Hence, on
{\mathcal{U}}
, we can express

(9.2)

\Psi^{(l)}|_{\mathcal{U}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\nu_{i}\log|z_{i}|^{2}+\phi,\qquad\phi\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{U}}),\quad\nu_{i}\in{\mathbf{R}}.

Here
e_{i}=0
implies
\nu_{i}=0
.
By (
9.1
), we have

[\widetilde{\kappa}_{Z,l}]|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}=[\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\widetilde{\kappa}_{X^{\prime}}+\kappa_{T})]|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}
.

In Proposition
4.2
, as
\widetilde{\kappa}_{X^{\prime}}
, we consider a sqeuence of Kähler forms

\{\kappa_{X^{\prime},k}\}
constructed in the same way as above from
\beta^{*}\kappa_{X}
. Applying the above construction with
initial Kähler form
\widetilde{\kappa}_{X^{\prime}}=\kappa_{X^{\prime},k}
and setting
\kappa_{Z,k}=\widetilde{\kappa}_{Z,l(k)}
with

l(k)
sufficiently large, we obtain a sequence of Kähler forms
\{\kappa_{Z,k}\}
used in Proposition
4.2
.
By the same argument as above,
\kappa_{X^{\prime},k}
can be expressed as
\kappa_{X^{\prime},k}=\beta^{*}\kappa_{X}+dd^{c}\psi^{(k)}
,
where
\psi^{(k)}
is a smooth function on
X^{\prime}\setminus X^{\prime}_{0}
with
\psi^{(k)}=1
on
X^{\prime}\setminus\pi^{\prime-1}(S(\frac{1}{k}))

and
\psi^{(k)}
admits an expression similar to (
9.2
) near
X^{\prime}_{0}
. Consequently, we can express

(9.3)

\kappa_{Z,k}=\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\beta^{*}\kappa_{X}+\kappa_{T})+dd^{c}\Phi_{k},

where
\Phi_{k}\in C^{\infty}(Z\setminus Z_{0})
admits the following expression near
Z_{0}=\varpi^{-1}(0)
:

(9.4)

\Phi_{k}|_{\mathcal{U}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\nu^{\prime}_{i}\log|z_{i}|^{2}+\phi^{\prime},\qquad\phi^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{U}}),\quad\nu^{\prime}_{i}\in{\mathbf{R}}.

Since
\Phi_{k}|_{Z\setminus Z_{0}}
is a smooth function on
Z\setminus Z_{0}
,
we get (
4.6
) by (
4.4
), (
9.3
).

9.2.
Takegoshi’s theorem with respect to the degenerate Kähler form

Takegoshi’s theorem (Theorem
2.1
) for
Z
with respect to the degenerate Kähler form

\kappa_{Z}=\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\beta^{*}\kappa_{X}+\kappa_{T})
was obtained by
Mourougane and Takayama
[
36
, Prop. 4.4]
.
However, it is not immediate from the proof if the operator
L^{q}
in
[
36
, Prop. 4.4]
is given by
the multiplication by
\kappa_{Z}^{q}
. To clarify this point, we prove the following:

Proposition 9.1
.

For any
u\in H^{q}(Z,K_{Z}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))
, there exists
\sigma\in\Gamma(Z,\Omega_{Z}^{n+1-q}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))
with

u=[\sigma\wedge\kappa_{Z}^{q}]
,
(\varpi^{*}dt)\wedge\sigma=0
and
D^{\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi,\widetilde{F}^{*}h_{\xi}}\sigma=0
.

Proof.

We write
Z_{0}=\varpi^{-1}(0)=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}D_{\lambda}
.
Then there is a Kähler form
\kappa_{Z,k}
on
Z
of the form
\kappa_{Z,k}=\kappa_{Z}+dd^{c}\Phi_{k}
with

\Phi_{k}\in C^{\infty}(Z\setminus Z_{0})
.
By Theorem
2.1
, there exists
\sigma\in\Gamma(Z,\Omega_{Z}^{n+1-q}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))
such that

u=[\sigma\wedge\kappa_{Z,k}^{q}]
,
(\varpi^{*}dt)\wedge\sigma=0
and
D^{\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi,\widetilde{F}^{*}h_{\xi}}\sigma=0
.
Since

\sigma\wedge(\kappa_{Z,k}^{q}-\kappa_{Z}^{q})=\sigma\wedge(\kappa_{Z,k}-\kappa_{Z})\wedge\sum_{i+j=q-1}\kappa_{Z,k}^{i}\kappa_{Z}^{j}
,
it suffices to prove the equality of cohomology classes
[\sigma\wedge(\kappa_{Z,k}-\kappa_{Z})]=0
on
Z
.
Since
\sigma\wedge(\kappa_{Z,k}-\kappa_{Z})=\frac{-1}{2\pi i}\bar{\partial}\{\sigma\wedge\partial\Phi_{k}\}
,
it suffices to prove that
\sigma\wedge\partial\Phi_{k}
is a smooth differential form on
Z
.

Set
E:=\bigcup_{\lambda\not=\lambda^{\prime}}D_{\lambda}\cap D_{\lambda^{\prime}}
and
D_{\lambda}^{o}:=D_{\lambda}\setminus E
.
Let
p\in D_{\lambda}^{o}
. There is a system of coordinates
({\mathcal{U}},(z_{0},\ldots,z_{n}))
of
Z
centered at
p
such that

\varpi(z)=\epsilon(z)\,z_{0}^{e_{\lambda}}
,
e_{\lambda}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{>0}
and
\epsilon(z)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{*}({\mathcal{U}})
.
Since
\varpi(z)=\epsilon(z)\,z_{0}^{e_{\lambda}}
and hence

\varpi^{*}dt=e_{\lambda}\epsilon(z)z_{0}^{e_{\lambda}-1}dz_{0}+z_{0}^{e_{\lambda}}d\epsilon
,
the condition
(\varpi^{*}dt)\wedge\sigma=0
implies that

(9.5)

\frac{dz_{0}}{z_{0}}\wedge\sigma=-\frac{1}{e_{\lambda}\epsilon(z)}d\epsilon(z)\wedge\sigma\in\Gamma({\mathcal{U}},\Omega_{Z}^{n+2-q}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi)).

Let
p\in E
. There is a system of coordinates
({\mathcal{U}},(z_{0},\ldots,z_{n}))
of
Z
centered at
p
such that

\varpi(z)=z_{0}^{e_{0}}\cdots z_{n}^{e_{n}}
,
e_{i}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.
If
e_{i}>0
, then

(\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}\wedge\sigma)|_{{\mathcal{U}}\setminus E}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{U}}\setminus E,\Omega_{Z}^{n+2-q}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))

by (
9.5
). Since
E
has codimension
2
in
{\mathcal{U}}
, the Riemann extension theorem implies that, if
e_{i}>0
,
then

(9.6)

\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}\wedge\sigma\in\Gamma({\mathcal{U}},\Omega_{Z}^{n+2-q}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi)).

Let
p\in Z_{0}
and let
({\mathcal{U}},(z_{0},\ldots,z_{n}))
be system of coordinates as above.
On
{\mathcal{U}}
, we may suppose that
\Phi_{k}
is expressed as in (
9.4
).
By (
9.4
), (
9.6
), we get

\sigma\wedge\partial\Phi_{k}|_{\mathcal{U}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\nu^{\prime}_{i}\,\sigma\wedge\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}+\sigma\wedge\partial\phi^{\prime}\in A^{n+2-q,0}({\mathcal{U}},\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi),

where
\nu^{\prime}_{i}=0
if
e_{i}=0
. Since
p\in Z_{0}
is arbitrary,
\sigma\wedge\partial\Phi_{k}\in A^{n+2-q,0}(Z,\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi)
.
∎

We are now in a position to prove Takegoshi’s theorem (Theorem
2.1
) for
(Y,F^{*}\xi)
with respect to
the degenerate Kähler form
\kappa_{Y}=\rho^{*}(\beta^{*}\kappa_{X}+\kappa_{T})
.

Proposition 9.2
.

For any
v\in H^{q}(Y,K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi))
, there exists a holomorphic form
\tau\in\Gamma(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{n+1-q}(F^{*}\xi))

with
v=[\tau\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}]
,
(f^{*}dt)\wedge\tau=0
and
D^{F^{*}\xi,F^{*}h_{\xi}}\tau=0
.

Proof.

Set
u:=\varphi^{*}v\in H^{q}(Z,K_{Z}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))
.
By Proposition
9.1
, there exists
\sigma\in\Gamma(Z,\Omega_{Z}^{n+1-q}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))
with
u=[\sigma\wedge\kappa_{Z}^{q}]
,
(\varpi^{*}dt)\wedge\sigma=0
and
D^{\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi,\widetilde{F}^{*}h_{\xi}}\sigma=0
.
Since
\varphi\colon Z\to Y
is a proper modification, there exists
\tau\in\Gamma(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{n+1-q}(F^{*}\xi))
with

\varphi^{*}\tau=\sigma
. Since
\varphi^{*}\kappa_{Y}=\kappa_{Z}
, we get
\varphi^{*}(v-[\tau\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}])=0
.
Since the map
\varphi^{*}\colon H^{q}(Y,K_{Y}(F^{*}\xi))\to H^{q}(Z,K_{Z}(\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi))
is an isomorphism
(cf.
[
46
, Theorem I, II]
),
we get
v=[\tau\wedge\kappa_{Y}^{q}]
. Then the identities
(f^{*}dt)\wedge\tau=0
and
D^{F^{*}\xi,F^{*}h_{\xi}}\tau=0
follow from
the corresponding identities
\varpi^{*}dt\wedge\sigma=0
and
D^{\widetilde{F}^{*}\xi,\widetilde{F}^{*}h_{\xi}}\sigma=0
.
∎

9.3.
A formula for the Bott-Chern type currents

We keep the notation in Section
6
.
In Subsection
9.3
, we assume that
N
is a reduced smooth divisor on
M

and that
E={\mathcal{O}}_{M}(-N)
,
E^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}_{M}
.
Let
s\in\Gamma(M,[N])
be the section such that
\varphi(e)=e\otimes s
and
{\rm div}(s)=N
.
Let
\nu
be the normal bundle of
N
in
M
. Let
h_{E}
,
h_{E^{\prime}}
,
h_{\nu}
be Hermitian metrics on these bundles.
Suppose that
h_{E^{\prime}}
is the trivial metric. We set
h_{[N]}:=h_{E}^{-1}
.
In this setting, we provide an explicit formula for the Bott-Chern type current

\langle\widetilde{\rm ch}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle
and compare it with the Bott-Chern singular current of
Bismut-Gillet-Soulé
[
11
]
,
[
12
]
, which we denote by
T(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}},h_{\nu})
.

Theorem 9.3
.

For
k\geq 1
, set
a_{k}:=\frac{1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{j}

(k\geq 2)
and
a_{1}:=0
. Then

(9.7)

\langle\widetilde{\rm ch}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle={\rm Td}^{-1}([N],h_{[N]})\log\|s\|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\frac{a_{k}}{k!}c_{1}([N],h_{[N]})^{k}.

In particular, if
M
is compact and the metrics
h_{E}
,
h_{E^{\prime}}
,
h_{\nu}
verify Bismut’s Assumption (A), i.e.,

\|ds|_{N}\|=1
on
N
, then the following identity holds in
P^{M}_{N}/P^{M,0}_{N}

(9.8)

\langle\widetilde{\rm ch}(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}})\rangle\equiv T(\overline{E},\overline{E^{\prime}},h_{\nu})+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\frac{a_{k}}{k!}c_{1}([N],h_{[N]})^{k},

where
P^{M}_{N}
and
P^{M,0}_{N}
are the spaces of currents on
M
introduced in
[
12
, Def. 1.3]
.

Proof.

Set
F:=E\oplus E^{\prime}=[N]^{-1}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{M}
and
h_{F}:=h_{E}\oplus h_{E^{\prime}}
. We set
\overline{[N]}:=([N],h_{[N]})
as well.
Let
U\subset M
be a small open subset and let
{\mathbf{e}}\in\Gamma(U,E)
be a nowhere vanishing section.
We fix a holomorphic trivialization
F|_{U}\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{U}^{\oplus 2}
given by
(v_{1}{\mathbf{e}},v_{2})\mapsto(v_{1},v_{2})
.
On the open subset of
{\mathbf{P}}(F(1))|_{U\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}
defined by
v_{1}\not=0
,
\zeta\not=0,\infty
,

{\mathscr{U}}={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbf{P}}(F(1))}(-1)
is generated by the section

(x,\zeta,v)\mapsto([({\mathbf{e}}(x)\otimes\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta),v\sigma_{0}(\zeta))],({\mathbf{e}}(x)\otimes\sigma_{\infty}(\zeta),v\sigma_{0}(\zeta)))
,
where
[({\mathbf{e}}\otimes\sigma_{\infty},v\sigma_{0})]\in{\mathbf{P}}(F(1))|_{U\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}
is the point corresponding to

({\mathbf{e}}\otimes\sigma_{\infty},v\sigma_{0})\in F(1)|_{U\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}
.
By (
6.2
),
\sigma_{\varphi}^{*}{\mathscr{U}}|_{U\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}
is generated by the section

([\varepsilon_{\varphi}({\mathbf{e}})],\varepsilon_{\varphi}({\mathbf{e}}))
with

\varepsilon_{\varphi}({\mathbf{e}})=({\mathbf{e}}\otimes\sigma_{\infty},\varphi({\mathbf{e}})\sigma_{0})
.
Set
u:=1/\zeta
and
\omega_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}:=\frac{i}{2\pi}\frac{d\zeta\wedge d\bar{\zeta}}{(1+|\zeta|^{2})^{2}}=\frac{i}{2\pi}\frac{du\wedge d\bar{u}}{(1+|u|^{2})^{2}}
.
Since
s=\varphi({\mathbf{e}}){\mathbf{e}}^{-1}
, we have

(9.9)

h_{\mathscr{U}}([\varepsilon_{\varphi}({\mathbf{e}})],\varepsilon_{\varphi}({\mathbf{e}}))=\frac{\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}+|\varphi({\mathbf{e}})|^{2}|\zeta|^{2}}{1+|\zeta|^{2}}=\frac{\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}}{1+|u|^{2}}(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2}).

Set
\theta:=\partial\log\|s\|^{2}
and
\Theta:=\bar{\partial}\partial\log\|s\|^{2}
. Then
c_{1}(\overline{[N]})=\frac{i}{2\pi}\Theta
.
Since

\bar{\partial}\partial\log(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})=\frac{\|s\|^{2}}{(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})^{2}}(d\bar{u}-\bar{u}\bar{\theta}){\scriptstyle\wedge}(du-u\theta)+\frac{\|s\|^{2}}{|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2}}\Theta

and
\bar{\partial}\partial\log\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}=-\bar{\partial}\partial\log\|s\|^{2}=-\Theta
, we obtain

(9.10)

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\bar{\partial}\partial\log\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}+\bar{\partial}\partial\log(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{1+\frac{\|s\|^{2}}{(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})^{2}}(d\bar{u}-\bar{u}\bar{\theta}){\scriptstyle\wedge}(du-u\theta)\right\}\exp\left\{-\frac{|u|^{2}}{|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2}}\Theta\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{1+\frac{\|s\|^{2}}{(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})^{2}}(d\bar{u}{\scriptstyle\wedge}du+|u|^{2}\bar{\theta}{\scriptstyle\wedge}\theta)+du{\scriptstyle\wedge}A+d\bar{u}{\scriptstyle\wedge}B\right\}\exp\left\{-\frac{|u|^{2}}{|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2}}\Theta\right\},

where
A
and
B
contain neither
du
nor
d\bar{u}
. By (
9.9
), (
9.10
), we get

(9.11)

\displaystyle\exp(c_{1}(\sigma_{\varphi}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{U}}))=\exp\left\{-dd^{c}\log\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}-dd^{c}\log(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})-\omega_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{1+\frac{i}{2\pi}\frac{\|s\|^{2}}{(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})^{2}}(d\bar{u}{\scriptstyle\wedge}du+|u|^{2}\theta{\scriptstyle\wedge}\theta)+du{\scriptstyle\wedge}A^{\prime}+d\bar{u}{\scriptstyle\wedge}B^{\prime}\right\}

\displaystyle\scriptstyle{\displaystyle\wedge}\exp\left\{-\frac{|u|^{2}}{|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2}}c_{1}(\overline{[N]})\right\}{\scriptstyle\wedge}(1-\omega_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}})

\displaystyle=-\omega_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}{\scriptstyle\wedge}\left(1+\frac{i}{2\pi}\frac{|u|^{2}\|s\|^{2}}{(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})^{2}}\theta{\scriptstyle\wedge}\bar{\theta}\right)\exp\left\{-\frac{|u|^{2}}{|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2}}c_{1}(\overline{[N]})\right\}

\displaystyle-\frac{\|s\|^{2}}{(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})^{2}}\frac{i}{2\pi}du{\scriptstyle\wedge}d\bar{u}{\scriptstyle\wedge}\exp\left\{-\frac{|u|^{2}}{|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2}}c_{1}(\overline{[N]})\right\}

\displaystyle+du{\scriptstyle\wedge}A^{\prime\prime}+d\bar{u}{\scriptstyle\wedge}B^{\prime\prime}+C^{\prime\prime},

where
A^{\prime}
,
B^{\prime}
,
A^{\prime\prime}
,
B^{\prime\prime}
,
C^{\prime\prime}
contain neither
du
nor
d\bar{u}
. Set
s=uw{\mathbf{e}}^{-1}
.
Then, on
q^{-1}(U\times{\mathbf{P}}^{1})
,
q^{-1}(N\times\{\infty\})
is defined locally by
u=w=0
.
By (
9.11
), we have

(9.12)

\displaystyle q^{*}\exp(c_{1}(\sigma_{\varphi}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{U}}))

\displaystyle=-\omega_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}\left(1+\frac{i}{2\pi}\frac{\frac{|w|^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}}}{(1+\frac{|w|^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}})^{2}}\partial\log\frac{|w|^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}}{\scriptstyle\wedge}\bar{\partial}\log\frac{|w|^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}}\right)\exp\left\{-\frac{c_{1}(\overline{[N]})}{1+\frac{|w|^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{e}}\|^{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle-\frac{\|s\|^{2}}{(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})^{2}}\frac{i}{2\pi}du{\scriptstyle\wedge}d\bar{u}{\scriptstyle\wedge}\exp\left\{-\frac{|u|^{2}}{|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2}}c_{1}(\overline{[N]})\right\}

\displaystyle+du{\scriptstyle\wedge}q^{*}A^{\prime\prime}+d\bar{u}{\scriptstyle\wedge}q^{*}B^{\prime\prime}+q^{*}C^{\prime\prime}.

Since
\int_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}\log|u|^{2}\omega_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}=0
, it follows from (
9.12
) that

(9.13)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}\log|\zeta|^{2}\cdot q^{*}\exp(c_{1}(\sigma_{\varphi}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{U}}))=-\int_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}\log|u|^{2}\cdot q^{*}\exp(c_{1}(\sigma_{\varphi}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{U}}))

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{P}}^{1}}\frac{\|s\|^{2}\log|u|^{2}}{(|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2})^{2}}\frac{i}{2\pi}du{\scriptstyle\wedge}d\bar{u}{\scriptstyle\wedge}\exp\left\{-\frac{|u|^{2}}{|u|^{2}+\|s\|^{2}}c_{1}(\overline{[N]})\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-c_{1}(\overline{[N]}))^{k}}{k!}\log\|s\|^{2}\int_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{|\zeta|^{2k}}{(1+|\zeta|^{2})^{k+2}}\frac{i}{2\pi}d\zeta d\bar{\zeta}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-c_{1}(\overline{[N]}))^{k}}{k!}\int_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{|\zeta|^{2k}\log|\zeta|^{2}}{(1+|\zeta|^{2})^{k+2}}\frac{i}{2\pi}d\zeta d\bar{\zeta}.

Since

\int_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{i}{2\pi}\frac{|\zeta|^{2k}d\zeta d\bar{\zeta}}{(1+|\zeta|^{2})^{k+2}}=\int_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{i}{2\pi}\frac{dud\bar{u}}{(1+|u|^{2})^{k+2}}=\frac{1}{k+1}
and

\int_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{|\zeta|^{2k}\log|\zeta|^{2}}{(1+|\zeta|^{2})^{k+2}}\frac{id\zeta d\bar{\zeta}}{2\pi}
is given by

-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\log\rho}{(1+\rho)^{k+1}}d\rho=a_{k}
,
(
9.7
) follows from (
9.13
).
When
h_{E}
,
h_{E^{\prime}}
,
h_{\nu}
verify Bismut’s Assumption (A), (
9.8
) is derived from
(
9.7
) and
[
11
, Rem. 3.5, especially (3.23), Th. 3.15, Th. 3.17]
.
∎
